# VerifiedOS project plan

## Purpose and scope

This is VerifiedOS's single backlog for reference bring-up, software, RTL,
assurance, qualification, tooling, research and upstream work. The
[requirements register](docs/requirements-register.md) owns obligations; the
[specification](docs/spec.md) explains the design; linked contracts define
interfaces and acceptance boundaries; the
[completion log](docs/implementation/completion-log.md) records accepted results.

The first release targets a general-purpose laptop: build the executable
reference, accept composed software at M8a and matching scalar RTL at M8b, then
complete separately scoped hardening and physical qualification. Statements and
experiments close only their stated scope; implementation and proof endpoints
retain their own work.

Prioritize the compiler-to-boot path and contracts that release its consumers.
Independent authoring starts when its own inputs and acceptance predicate are
available. Software and RTL have separate acceptance gates; optional
instantiations and deferred hardening keep the entry conditions in their items.
The detailed sequencing below controls those decisions.

## Plan maintenance

Task IDs are permanent. S, Q, M, R, I and U labels retain their identities,
estimates, dependencies and evidence. Under the
[checklist conventions](#checklist-conventions), unchecked means open, checked
means accepted at the stated boundary, and struck work retains its disposition.
Work-order data separates committed, conditional and unfunded work. Proposals
and research notes confer no implementation commission, priced-total membership
or completion credit.

Update tasks here as evidence or decisions change; keep rationale, requirements
and historical evidence in their linked owners. Supporting documents link here
for next actions. Generate arithmetic and dispatch views with
`python tools/run.py check --fix`, and the upstream backlog from its captured
snapshot with `python docs/implementation/upstream-prs/render.py`. Generated
statuses describe their recorded inputs and observation time. Follow
[the validation handoff](AGENTS.md#tool-execution-and-validation) for settled changes.

## Delivery approach

This non-normative companion to [spec.md](docs/spec.md) implements the reference
bring-up of [§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization). Unlinked §N references name
specification sections. [§0](#0-the-discipline-two-languages-two-golden-models)–
[§11](#11-building-an-fpga-from-it-all) define the milestones;
[§12](#12-build-order-milestones-and-execution-state) splits them into checkable
tasks at entry and tracks execution.

Generate the fast executable golden model directly from the two verification
languages before production optimization. Robust CompCert-CHERI preservation,
on-artifact constant-time verification, RTL ⊑ Sail and WCET certificates remain
hardening endpoints checked against that reference.

The Sail-generated **golden emulator** is the daily reference; scalar RTL under
Verilator is the second implementation ([§10](#10-emulating-the-hardware-reference-execution-and-rtl-comparison)).
M2 retains the retired QEMU work and reopening criterion. **M8a** accepts composed
reference software; **M8b** accepts matching scalar RTL co-simulation
([§11](#11-building-an-fpga-from-it-all)). RTL develops beside software and consumes
M7.1's image without waiting for M8a's separate measurements.

## 0. The discipline: two languages, two golden models

Bring-up uses Sail for the machine and Gallina for software definitions and proofs, with the C refinements and generated artifacts below. Each produces an executable reference through its stated extraction, refinement or generation path. The [Vela whole-system proposal](#vela-whole-system-authoring-proposal) targets single-language authorship, including replacement of Sail, after its acceptance and migration gates.

- **Hardware, Sail.** One machine model (ISA, datapath and modeled devices), parameterized by core class ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)), generates the golden emulator through Sail's C backend. R2 supplies the independent RTL executor under Verilator; the existing differential corpus and RVFI-DII rig adjudicate divergence against Sail. Later, the same source supports net-new **Kôika/Kami** blocks, imported cores' **Sail-SV + commercial-FEV** evidence and generated synthesis SystemVerilog ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)), plus Sail → Coq, Isla and the **one Iris-over-Sail program logic** ([§13](docs/spec.md#13-packaging--supply-chain)). These proof flows are deferred.
- **Software, Rocq/Coq (Gallina).** Every TCB and base-system component has one Gallina source, lowered for host checking and device execution. **Device lowering is GC-free:** a tracing GC adds an unverified runtime to the TCB and unbounded pauses to the WCET path ([§10](docs/spec.md#10-storage--state), [§11](docs/spec.md#11-updates)). `CertiCoq → Clight`, which emits a GC, is host-only.
  - **Host logic and oracles.** Author against reviewed contracts and check with `run.py proofs`, generated properties and mutations where they decide behavior; no target compiler or image is needed. Use **CertiCoq → Wasm** for required executable comparisons. M1.5 supplies the demonstration and M4.3 the staged IPC oracle; each additional consumer owns its staging. Host oracles test values and logic; target execution checks capability enforcement. No host runtime ships or enters device timing bounds.
  - **Device purecap artifacts.** Choose the Coq-native, GC-free route matching the component's memory behavior, without adding a collector or prover:
    - **Gallina → CompCert-C (VST/Iris) → CHERI-CompCert** for the [kernel](#5-kernel-coq), [M-mode firmware](#3-m-mode-firmware-coq) and heap-churning [object](#6-ostree-style-object-system--update-transactor-coq)/[filesystem](#7-filesystem-coq) layers. This is the specified CompCert/SECOMP end-state ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)); GC-free C precedes its deferred refinement proof.
    - **MetaCoq → Rust with `bumpalo` arenas → certifying Rust→RV64+CHERI** for allocation-light, arena-shaped components assigned to safe Rust ([§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization)); **MCQC** is the GC-free extraction precedent. The [admission checkers](#9-admission-checkers-cheri-tal-type-checker--coqmetacoq-proof-kernel) cannot use this route: nothing re-checks their binaries.
    - **Fiat/Bedrock synthesis → imperative Clight** for explicit-memory code, including Narcissus wire parsers ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)) and Fiat-Crypto [field arithmetic](#4-verified-crypto-core-coq).
    - **Vélus (Lustre → Clight)** for [server control planes](docs/spec.md#12-system-servers-contained-outside-the-tcb): Coq-verified, statically allocated, with structural WCET, determinism and causality ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)). Like Narcissus and Fiat-Crypto, this DSL adds no trust language: its semantics and compilation are Coq objects.

Validate the models independently, then compose: exercise Sail-C through the generated/differential campaigns and Gallina through proofs and required executable oracles; lower components GC-free to purecap RV64+CHERI and run them on Sail. The Sail-generated emulator is both reference and daily driver; S4 measures suitability, and M2 records the retired software fork.

**Shared component contract.** Specify each component in Gallina, perform its required proof/oracle checks, and lower GC-free to the frozen purecap target. The composed binary runs on the [emulator](#10-emulating-the-hardware-reference-execution-and-rtl-comparison), RTL co-simulation and [FPGA](#11-building-an-fpga-from-it-all). M1.6 keeps CompCert-C as the default; alternatives first pass their target and correspondence gates. Component entries give **Toolchain**, **Start from** and **Plan**, with exceptions for the Sail [processor](#1-the-processor-the-fusion-soc-sail) and [RoT](#2-root-of-trust-sail-scalar-rv64cheri-core--coq-firmware), [Lustre init](#8-init-system-the-static-supervision-tree-lustre-via-vélus) and [two-checker split](#9-admission-checkers-cheri-tal-type-checker--coqmetacoq-proof-kernel).

### The target-execution prerequisite: a functional CHERI-CompCert backend

M1.2 owns the functional purecap backend, starting from the pinned SECOMP tree's `riscV/` backend and retaining its compartment layer. M1.1b/M1.2a reject `cheririscV/` as a working translation. The backend stays in the contained compiler repository under M1.1a; correctness and robust-preservation proofs are deferred.

It gates **target execution**. Firmware, kernel C, storage logic, contracts, generators and harnesses can proceed against landed interfaces. M1.2b owns representation; M1.2g value/tagged-memory carriers; M1.2c permissions/sentries; M1.2e representability; and M1.2d frames, calling convention and primitives. R-05-023b's primitives, including `vmclear`, `fence.t`, `cspecialrw` and `csealentry`, must execute before kernel bring-up.

M1.2f joins the backend to the **landed M1.4′ assembler/composer** for component and generated-C comparisons. M1.7 tests and independently reproduces a purecap image. Downstream executables reuse this path; M1.4's deferred LLVM disassembly/object-tool re-homing adds no bring-up prerequisite.

Optimized scheduling/lowering (R-18-014a, M1.3), outlining/tail merging (M1.3a) and ordered final freeze (M1.8b) consume the functional backend without blocking its acceptance. Bound-directed lowering keeps R-18-014c's inputs. No purecap GC is built.

### The profile-freeze measurement instrument

Generated code-size inputs arrive after bring-up. One instrument spans two milestones:

1. **M0 defines the contract with the profile:** versioned freeze corpus and generated-source inputs, composition recipe, admitted region classes, per-choice thresholds, pre-assembly operand-class provenance schema, and report of realized dictionary, bytes and Sail worst-case cycles. Include the composed base image, generated Narcissus UPER RRC and IEI/TLV codecs, MMIO accessors, prologues, epilogues, calls and global-address materializations. The [profile-freeze measurement contract](docs/implementation/contracts/freeze-measurement.md) owns corpus membership, recipe, provenance strata, regions, thresholds, columns and CI predicates.
2. **M1.8b connects the landed M1.8a analyzer to producers and runs the final sweep.** Reuse M1.4′'s link map/site table and add backend provenance once, under the authored M1.8b contract amendments. Producer work may overlap backend development; the final report needs the accepted backend, outlined corpus and real composer outputs. Use [§1](#1-the-processor-the-fusion-soc-sail)'s Sail timing table for cycle deltas.

   Follow the contract's [decision order](docs/implementation/contracts/freeze-measurement.md#1-the-measured-act-what-is-decided-and-in-what-order). Each recipe composes/merges before dictionary selection (R-15-036i), reporting hit rate by operand class (R-15-036h, R-15-036k). Measure outlining/tail merging as bytes and worst-case cycles per admitted region (R-15-036o, R-15-036p); then PC-relative versus composition-time-absolute calls/addresses (R-15-036l), `bfext`/`bfins` (R-15-067d), the further code-size candidate (R-15-067e), indexed load/store scale immediate (R-15-007g), and emitted adjacency histogram for fusion-set selection (R-15-031a). The [§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform) exploration owns fusion admission.

   Multi-register single-check save/restore is a **nil row**, struck before freeze (R-15-036n): record the strike with byte/cycle columns `n/a`; a measured row would be an amendment. Decide `rcstep` on worst-case decoder cycles/frame over its conformance streams against the admitted media slot/decode ceiling (R-15-067h, R-15-238c), in the same report with byte columns `n/a`.

   Publish the corpus manifest, tool version, thresholds, per-extension deltas and choices. CI rejects missing required members, provenance strata, regions, byte or worst-case-cycle columns.

### Explicit scope cut (what this plan deliberately does **not** build yet)

The certifying Rust producer adapts the selected `upstream/cheri-rust` capability-aware rustc/LLVM pin to frozen RV64. [Its disposition](docs/background/architectural-alternatives.md#functional-cheri-rust-compiler-starting-point) retires a fresh functional CHERI port; safe-MIR correspondence, temporal-safety/typed-control-flow certificates, target completeness and bounded resident execution remain required. It is outside the priced checklist; source selection subtracts nothing from the CompCert backend or resident-port estimate.

Bring-up defers these [language](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification), [TCB](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive) and [realization](docs/spec.md#18-realization) workstreams:

- **CHERI-CompCert secure compilation/robust preservation.** Build the functional purecap backend now; defer the SECOMP2CHERI theorem that preserves compartment isolation against adversarial linked contexts.
- **Binary constant-time verification.** Crypto is a Gallina functional reference without a CT guarantee. CryptOpt-style field-arithmetic translation validation is deleted, rather than deferred, under [§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)'s rule against a new verified artifact for speed alone.
- **Certifying Rust certificate mode and Tier-2 memory-safety certificates** ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification), [§13](docs/spec.md#13-packaging--supply-chain)). Reference base components assigned to safe Rust use Gallina; contained-Rust userspace is deferred. Functional Rust lowering remains the MetaCoq→Rust arena target and a specified priority-zero item ([§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization)).
- **RTL ⊑ Sail and full VST(Iris) refinement** for [kernel](#5-kernel-coq), [objects](#6-ostree-style-object-system--update-transactor-coq) and [filesystem](#7-filesystem-coq). Author GC-free CompCert-C in the target proof shape; use Wasm/Sail differentials pending machine-checked artifact ⊑ Gallina proofs.
- **WCET cost annotation and interval-arithmetic cyclic-executive schedulability.** The pass belongs in the certifying toolchain over typed control-flow graphs, with no standalone estimator ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification), [§11](docs/spec.md#11-updates)). Bring-up measures emulator timing without certification.

These hardening layers replace reference components in place, checked against the golden model.

**Both static memory latency classes are in scope** ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform), R-15-247). M0.14 models both constants and M0.16 the refresh/discharge sequencer as [§12](docs/spec.md#12-system-servers-contained-outside-the-tcb) matter, before C-class freeze. Fit the roster to both classes from that freeze. R5 defers physical qualification (R-15-247m), and M9a composition-capacity droop closure; adding a class after a one-class freeze would reopen it.

---

## 1. The processor: the fusion SoC (Sail)

One die replaces the GPU/accelerator with ISA-visible cores in one class-parameterized Sail model ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)): **V-class** long vectors handle rasterization, compositing, codecs and ISP; **M-class** matrices handle GEMM/AI. Both share the scalar front end, differing in VLEN/matrix geometry; there is no fixed-function GPU.

- **Language:** Sail.
- **Toolchain:** Sail's C emulator backend (`sail -c`); OCaml for a quick interpreter. Sail → Coq and **Isla** symbolic execution are deferred proof tools.
- **Start from:** the booting RV64+CHERI models in `riscv/sail-riscv` and `CTSRD-CHERI/sail-cheri-riscv`, curated and extended together.
- **Compiler target:** portable Sail-C compiled by host `clang`/`gcc` to `x86-64`/`arm64`; the emulated ISA is purecap **RV64IMV + CHERI**, curated to [§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform).
- **Plan:**
  1. **Curate the [§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform) profile.** The [frozen instruction-set profile](docs/hardware/isa-profile.md) owns base/ABI, adopted and excluded extensions with grounds, CHERI features, custom/frozen units and CSR bank, each traced to requirements. Make `sail-riscv` ⋈ `sail-cheri-riscv` match it; this freeze gates the schedule ([§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization)). `Zabha` already exists as extension-gated byte/halfword cases in `sail-riscv`'s `Zaamo` file and needs no new mechanism.
  2. **Adopt Ztso/RVTSO and static-only prediction** ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)), with no dynamic predictor state. Store-buffer deletion, *sequential consistency by absence*, remains the DSE candidate in [architectural alternatives](docs/background/architectural-alternatives.md).
  3. **Parameterize by class.** Add V-class RVV (VLEN=4096) and a fork-and-frozen M-class systolic-GEMM extension. Scalar-issued vector/matrix memory operations are capability-checked, per element for gather/scatter. LDPC/polar **FEC** and optional Keccak are fixed-geometry, core-issued capability-operand instructions with no DMA or firmware.
  4. **Model two static memory latencies** ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform), R-15-247). Put per-class density, retention interval, bank count and latency constants in `platform_config` and the devicetree schema. Model the refresh/discharge sequencer as a fixed-function register slave with no fetch ([§12](docs/spec.md#12-system-servers-contained-outside-the-tcb)); add no instruction, access type or decode surface (M0.14, M0.16).
  5. **Annotate timing:** fixed-latency DIV/FPU/AMO, mask-independent vectors and static fetch ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)). These prepare the same source for WCET/CT/Ztso proofs; bring-up treats them as documentation checked by measurement.
  6. **Generate and freeze the emulator** as the executable ISA reference for every software image below.

### Choosing the frozen parameters: a proof-aware design-space exploration

Off-model **DSE** selects VLEN/matrix geometry, class core counts, issue width/pipeline depth, SRAM bank/macro/tier-to-island mapping, scratchpad sizes, FEC geometry and TDM-NoC schedule. There are no hardware caches or integrity tree to size ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)). Its objectives are performance, area, power, WCET and **proof simplicity**.

Three constraints bound the search: R-15-163's conditional materials grading supplies the external limit on first-class vertical tiers; R-15-007q binds CBO size to tag groups and both SRAM-macro and bulk deck row/page geometry (M0.13 supplies the feasible interval); and R-15-247g's second-class droop envelope is a hard constraint, while island bandwidth and bitline-dependent read energy are objectives on bank count (M0.17).

- **Proof cost.** Score estimated Sail surface, decode/state complexity and per-candidate refinement effort as inexpensive proxies for the deferred [RTL ⊑ Sail](#11-building-an-fpga-from-it-all) work ([§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization)).
- **Admission.** Prune candidates failing the five-part admission test or non-interference/schedulability obligations ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform), [§8](docs/spec.md#8-authority-model), [§11](docs/spec.md#11-updates)). The chosen frozen configuration still needs its per-class RTL ⊑ Sail proof; proxy quality affects search, not soundness.
- **Tools and staging.** An NSGA-II-class optimizer or SMT/ILP feasibility oracle wraps the admission predicate and parameterized cost model. Its output is re-modeled in Sail and checked; the optimizer is untrusted ([§0](#0-the-discipline-two-languages-two-golden-models), [§6](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive)). Bring-up uses hand-chosen parameters; DSE follows the Sail cost model and per-class RTL, with the other [hardening](#11-building-an-fpga-from-it-all).

The exploration retains four explicit questions:

1. **Store-buffer deletion.** [Architectural alternatives](docs/background/architectural-alternatives.md) keeps sequential consistency by absence open. Average throughput ratio **ρ** cannot establish zero-wait service: grants `[2, 0]` with peak demand one operation/cycle give ρ = 1 but a second-phase arrival waits. Require a per-phase proof or interval service bound under actual static issue constraints, covering every permitted arrival, bank conflict, write occupancy, refresh slot and residual pipeline operation on both classes. Compare completion/visibility order, WCET, switch drain, area and power. Retain the TSO buffer and `Ztso` until this predicate and the full deletion checklist pass.

   Re-evaluate when R-15-108 issue/memory-issue widths, R-11-017 schedule, R-15-228 grants and R-15-247m qualified occupancy/timing exist; missing inputs leave it open. A schedule may restrict arrivals only if emitted code is checked against that restriction. R-15-218/R-15-220 own drain cost, R-17-041 its unmeasured magnitude, Q22e the bounded comparison, and datapath/refinement owners any selected implementation.
2. **Product floor.** R-18-004a's eight-workload general-purpose laptop floor and R-18-004b's per-class capacity/bandwidth/area comparisons are hard constraints under R-15-109a. The elastic desktop pool counts in second-class demand. R-18-004d fixes demonstrations before candidates are known. The five comparisons include area fit against core count/issue width on R-15-108a's fixed planar area and both bandwidth floors against class bank counts. R-15-162/R-15-163/R-18-004 retain costs; R-17-053b lists forfeits.
3. **Masked datapath order and width.** Select sharing order *d* and width (R-15-108) for R-05-004a's dedicated parametric-order HPC datapath; R-05-070 rejects secret operands on R-15-055 vector crypto. Order and nonlinear gates/cycle set DRBG expansion rate (R-15-241) and the crypto [slot budget](docs/spec.md#11-updates); width sets the per-extent AEAD (R-10-022) and link-encryption (R-12-039) ceiling. Search both with expander area/power, carrying R-17-058d ineffective-fault redundancy in every candidate. Narrower width worsens R-17-058a's per-share-noise residual.
4. **Per-class capacity split.** R-18-004b fixes payload floors with ECC/tags additional, each class's exclusivity bound (R-15-172), demand from resident bytes in R-15-247s's two lists, and second-class M-island/aggregate bandwidth targets. R-15-247p bank counts must meet these floors; M0.17 bandwidth is an objective above them. M1.9 still determines which regions need cycle-critical placement; floors alone do not.

---

## 2. Root of Trust (Sail scalar RV64+CHERI core + Coq firmware)

The on-die OpenTitan-class RoT is the sole management processor: scalar RV64+CHERI core, TRNG, OTP/key store, monotonic counters and watchdog ([§9](docs/spec.md#9-boot--root-of-trust), [§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)). It shares the [main-die purecap format](#1-the-processor-the-fusion-soc-sail), Sail model and CHERI-CompCert backend, in a minimal profile without C or V/M. This lets the capability-forest root mint/measure the main die's RV64 capabilities and address multi-GiB memory directly, avoiding a 32-bit CHERIoT format seam and 4 GiB boot windowing. The golden model uses a second, smaller Sail core with Gallina firmware.

- **Language:** Sail core/peripherals, host-emulated by Sail-C; Gallina firmware.
- **Toolchain:** heap-free, mostly static CompCert-C through CHERI-CompCert, targeting scalar RV64+CHERI without V/M.
- **Start from:** `lowRISC/opentitan` and Ibex functional/RTL references; CHERIoT-Ibex (Sonata) demonstrates CHERI on an Ibex-class core. Configure `sail-cheri-riscv` from scalar RV64IMC+CHERI to RV64IM+CHERI, without C or V/M, using the main-die format. Model OTP, seeded-PRNG TRNG, counters and windowed watchdog as Sail MMIO devices.
- **Plan:** implement measured boot, seal/unseal, attestation quotes and anti-rollback counters in Gallina, with PQ signatures from the [crypto core](#4-verified-crypto-core-coq). The RoT boots first, measures M-mode firmware and releases the main die, composing the [boot chain](docs/spec.md#9-boot--root-of-trust) as one emulator driving another.

---

## 3. M-mode firmware (Coq)

Minimal firmware is quiescent after boot ([§6](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive), [§7](docs/spec.md#7-kernel)). In single Machine mode, the kernel takes traps; there is no delegation or SMM-style resident handler.

- **Toolchain:** heap-free **CompCert-C → CHERI-CompCert** under the [shared contract](#0-the-discipline-two-languages-two-golden-models).
- **Start from:** fresh Gallina, with OpenSBI only as a functional checklist for initial capabilities and boot handoff; omit PMP setup ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)).
- **Plan:** derive each core's partition-bounded root and read-execute-only kernel/firmware text capabilities (W^X, [§14](docs/spec.md#14-userland)). Use CHERI alone, without PMP/`Smepmp` ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)); launch one M-mode kernel per core. Lower GC-free to purecap RV64+CHERI: this is the main die's first image after RoT release.

---

## 4. Verified crypto core (Coq)

Provides boot verification, attestation, sealing and storage AEAD ([§6](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive), item 2; [§10](docs/spec.md#10-storage--state)). Production uses verified C through CHERI-CompCert, including field arithmetic, on-artifact constant-time/masking proofs, *d*-probing/composition theorems (R-05-004a, R-15-053a) and SSProve/FCF reductions ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)). CryptOpt is deleted by that section's rule. Bring-up is a Gallina functional reference without CT, masking or reduction guarantees.

- **Toolchain:** GC-free CompCert-C through CHERI-CompCert (or GC-free extraction), retaining the production field-arithmetic route; on-artifact CT verification is deferred ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)).
- **Start from:** Coq-native **Fiat-Crypto** for classical fields; Gallina references for ML-KEM/ML-DSA, SHA-2/3 and AES-GCM/ChaCha20-Poly1305. Use FIPS 203/204/205 vectors and installed OpenSSL's behavior as the oracle for ML-KEM-1024/ML-DSA-87, absent from the pinned `HACL*`/libjade editions.
  No `F*`/Z3 dependency enters the golden model; correctness testing precedes reduction/CT proofs.
- **Plan:** expose hash, AEAD seal/open, ML-KEM encaps/decaps, ML-DSA sign/verify and a RoT-TRNG-seeded DRBG. Run known-answer tests through Wasm. Consumers are the [RoT](#2-root-of-trust-sail-scalar-rv64cheri-core--coq-firmware), [object system](#6-ostree-style-object-system--update-transactor-coq) and [filesystem](#7-filesystem-coq).
- **Masked end-state:** R-05-004a's dedicated parametric-order *d* datapath, separate from the eight-lane VLEN=4096 vector crypto units. Start with HPC lineage (SMAesH, COMPRESS, OpenTitan first-order DOM AES/KMAC and stream-cipher randomness expansion). Use untrusted SILVER exact gadget checks, PROLEAD composed statistical checks, MATCHI composition and Coco-class execution-aware netlist verification (R-15-053a), plus VERICA/FIVER-class combined verification for R-17-058d ineffective-fault protection, selected with masking.

  Required artifacts are the Coq probing model, composition theorems and R-17-058d reduction. The [proof inventory](docs/assurance/proof-reuse/crypto.md) qualifies no complete compatible source: the Lean arithmetic source proves a cardinality statement still needing probability/order/leakage correspondence; EasyCrypt gadget proofs inform the Boolean half; combined reduction has no qualified reusable source. Post-M10 cells price these distinct source-qualification and mathematical obligations; cross-prover reuse exceeds transcription. The DRBG's computational security is a named assumption in R-05-004a's expansion role, and ML-DSA uses hedged signing on that path.

---

## 5. Kernel (Coq)

One bespoke seL4-inspired capability kernel per core ([§7](docs/spec.md#7-kernel)): endpoints/notifications, partition contexts, static cyclic executive (no MCS), CHERI epoch/load-filter revocation over a kernel-owned grant table ([§8](docs/spec.md#8-authority-model)), and CHERIoT-lineage switching/sealing/interrupt sentries ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)). It has one address space, no MMU/VSpace/page-table objects, untyped memory, capability space or derivation tree ([§7](docs/spec.md#7-kernel), [§8](docs/spec.md#8-authority-model)).

Removing VM, MCS, SMP, S/U modes and PMP/IOMMU avoids `l4v`'s hardest, least-maintained layers; removing retype, capability lookup and CDT revocation leaves the novel CHERI obligations. [The seL4/CertiKOS and object-model rationale](docs/background/inspirations.md) records the design basis.

- **Toolchain:** GC-free CompCert-C refined against Gallina with VST/Iris and compiled through CHERI-CompCert, following the specified seL4/CertiKOS CompCert/SECOMP method ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)). No post-boot allocation means no heap or collector ([§7](docs/spec.md#7-kernel)).
- **Start from:** seL4 endpoint/non-interference design, ABI and object model for surviving types. Under M4.1a, author Gallina from this repository's design, translating no seL4 Haskell artifact; the local specification and review supply assurance under the repository's licence. Omit VSpace/paging and MCS from initial authorship. Cross-check CHERI single-address-space/lifetime behavior against CheriOS reservation/claim and CHERIoT revocation ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)). Bring-up needs the executable model before its proofs.
- **Plan:**
  1. Author the surviving objects, endpoint/notification IPC, partition context and cyclic schedule in Gallina. Exclude VSpace/page-table/frame mappings ([§7](docs/spec.md#7-kernel)), MCS scheduling contexts ([§11](docs/spec.md#11-updates)), untyped/retype, capability-space and derivation-tree classes ([§8](docs/spec.md#8-authority-model)). State revocation against CHERI epochs, grants and load filters; M4.1 selects R-08-007's incremental preemptible form, dropping quiescent points.
  2. Exercise create/derive/revoke, IPC round-trips and slot-overrun faults through the host Wasm oracle.
  3. Author GC-free CompCert-C against Gallina under M4.1b. CHERI-Alliance CHERI-seL4/CHERI-Microkit's kernel is GPL-2.0-only, including its CHERI work, so that maintained-code start is unavailable for this Apache-2.0 composition. Compile through CHERI-CompCert and boot one sequential kernel instance per emulated core with disjoint state and partition-bounded CHERI roots, without PMP ([§7](docs/spec.md#7-kernel)).
  4. Defer functional refinement (C ⊑ Gallina) and non-interference proofs ([§8](docs/spec.md#8-authority-model)). They layer onto the same spec/C; purecap CHERI-C refinement is the early kill-switch, followed by multikernel non-interference composition. There is no CDT proof ([rationale](docs/background/inspirations.md)). Purecap, GC-free compilation is required from first boot by the [backend prerequisite](#0-the-discipline-two-languages-two-golden-models).

---

## 6. OSTree-style object system + update transactor (Coq)

A content-addressed Merkle-DAG store ([§10](docs/spec.md#10-storage--state)) and atomic update transactor ([§6](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive), item 3) provide read-verification against the boot-attested signed root, A/B generations and monotonic anti-rollback.

- **Toolchain:** GC-free CompCert-C (VST/Iris) through CHERI-CompCert, sharing the [filesystem](#7-filesystem-coq) codebase under [§10](docs/spec.md#10-storage--state)'s no-runtime rule.
- **Start from:** `libostree` concepts (content addressing, A/B deploy, rollback), with fresh Gallina store/transactor. Use the [crypto module](#4-verified-crypto-core-coq) for hashes/signatures and share storage L0/L1 and their journal with the [filesystem](#7-filesystem-coq).
- **Plan:** implement object hashes/Merkle links, signed-root verification on every read, A/B commit (stage → verify → flip → fall back on health failure), and a RoT-counter-sealed anti-rollback floor ([§9](docs/spec.md#9-boot--root-of-trust), [§11](docs/spec.md#11-updates)). Bring-up stubs proof-checked admission to signature/hash checks pending the [device proof checker](#9-admission-checkers-cheri-tal-type-checker--coqmetacoq-proof-kernel).

---

## 7. Filesystem (Coq)

The [four-layer stack](docs/spec.md#10-storage--state) composes Gallina modules for L0 journal, L1 CoW B-tree index, L2 filesystem semantics and L3 confidentiality. Bring-up retains production's GC-free CompCert-C shape, using Wasm differentials while deferring full VST/Iris refinement proofs and completing the CompCert-C/VST re-homing.

- **Toolchain:** GC-free CompCert-C (VST/Iris) through CHERI-CompCert, against a modeled block device ([§10](docs/spec.md#10-storage--state)).
- **Start from:** Coq-native **RefFS** for L2 concurrent linearizability/liveness (MoLi), **SFSCQ/DiskSec** for L3 data non-interference. [RefFS's OSDI 2024 paper](https://www.usenix.org/system/files/osdi24-zou.pdf) excludes crash safety and leaves mechanized MoLi soundness open; crash refinement needs (S)FSCQ/Perennial lineage and a separate composition proof. The [reuse inventory](docs/assurance/proof-reuse/systems.md) records editions and transfer limits.

  Re-express Dafny **VeriBetrFS**'s B^ε-tree as L1 Gallina and **Perennial/GoJournal**'s journal design as L0 Gallina (its proof is Coq, code Go via Goose). Read the selected Perennial crash weakest preconditions, recovery/refinement lemmas and record transfer to this crash model before duplicating machinery. Goose's trusted translation/Go model supplies no Gallina/source or CHERI connection; add no Go runtime or second authoritative semantics. Use [crypto](#4-verified-crypto-core-coq) for per-extent AEAD.
- **Plan:** compose L0 journal, parametric L1 CoW B-tree instantiated per object class, L2 typed keys/snapshot-version-in-key/RefFS semantics, and L3 per-domain AEAD/non-interference. Exercise Wasm over an in-memory disk; lower GC-free through CompCert-C/VST and CHERI-CompCert to purecap on the emulator's modeled block device.

  Use the same L1 for metadata/secondary-index keys and one L0 transaction for objects, metadata and indexes. Derive bounded live-query deltas from committed typed-key changes; test confidentiality-domain/namespace-capability scope, crash atomicity, ordered delivery and overflow-to-rescan. Build the [system-integrity/transactor store](#6-ostree-style-object-system--update-transactor-coq) before the bcachefs-class user-data instance on the same codebase ([§10](docs/spec.md#10-storage--state)). Defer replication, EC, tiering, copygc and FTL to the safe-Rust availability workstream.

---

## 8. Init system: the static supervision tree (Lustre via Vélus)

A static supervision tree provides declarative units, no ambient authority and capability re-grants after restart ([§12](docs/spec.md#12-system-servers-contained-outside-the-tcb), [§16](docs/spec.md#16-reliability)). Its synchronous control plane has structural WCET, determinism and causality, with a Gallina differential oracle ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification), [§12](docs/spec.md#12-system-servers-contained-outside-the-tcb)).

- **Language:** Lustre control plane, the [shared Gallina contract](#0-the-discipline-two-languages-two-golden-models)'s exception; Gallina model for the Wasm oracle.
- **Toolchain:** Coq-verified **Vélus (Lustre → Clight) → CHERI-CompCert**, with statically allocated nodes and no arena or GC.
- **Start from:** fresh Lustre using systemd unit/supervision shape without ambient authority ([§12](docs/spec.md#12-system-servers-contained-outside-the-tcb)) and Erlang/OTP static-tree restart/backoff semantics. Consume compiled, typed, signed configuration objects, without runtime text parsing ([§10](docs/spec.md#10-storage--state)).
- **Plan:** model the signed-generation component graph, ordered capability-granting startup, crash detection, restart/backoff and re-grant. Consume [object-system](#6-ostree-style-object-system--update-transactor-coq) configuration and [kernel](#5-kernel-coq) capability operations. Init is the first kernel process and starts the remaining reference components.

---

## 9. Admission checkers (CHERI-TAL type-checker ⋈ Coq/MetaCoq proof kernel)

Two stratified checkers serve [§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification), [§6](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive) and [§13](docs/spec.md#13-packaging--supply-chain):

- The decidable **CHERI-TAL checker**, order-10³ lines, validates every installed binary's typing derivation against spec/Sail versions: Tier-2 memory safety, CFI, no-codegen and ABI/type conformance.
- The larger **CIC kernel** (MetaCoq lineage) validates every binary's artifact-local source-correspondence theorem, plus deep Tier-0/hyperproperty proofs, predominantly over the base-image TCB at release: refinement, non-interference, crypto reduction, filesystem, and residual unstructured CT/WCET. Structured CT/WCET are CHERI-TAL type properties ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)).

Their golden models are essentially their production forms.

- **Language:** Gallina proof-term/type-derivation checkers. Prove CHERI-TAL's well-typed ⇒ Sail-safe metatheorem once in Coq, following foundational TAL/RustBelt/WasmCert-Coq.
- **Toolchain:** **VST-refined CompCert-C → CHERI-CompCert** for both, as for [kernel](#5-kernel-coq) and [storage](#6-ostree-style-object-system--update-transactor-coq)/[filesystem](#7-filesystem-coq). Nothing re-checks their binaries, so [unverified MetaCoq→Rust and Rust→CHERI](#0-the-discipline-two-languages-two-golden-models) are inadmissible. Use explicit-memory arenas (check one proof, wipe the arena) covered by VST; no GC or unverified extraction.

  The verified **CertiCoq → Clight → CompCert** route assembles a MetaRocq checker with **VeriFFI**'s Coq↔C boundary and **CertiGC**, but remains host-side and a documented fallback if full CIC VST refinement fails. Even a verified GC adds a runtime carve-out to the bootstrap root ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)). VeriFFI generates boundary artifacts without a composed theorem: its paper reports no demonstrated CertiCoq end-to-end theorem and no stated theorem for open programs with foreign functions. A fallback must price that hypothesized soundness link. The selected refined-C device checker has no Coq↔C boundary.
- **Start from:** MetaCoq's verified Gallina checker as the CIC C refinement specification, inheriting in-Coq soundness ([§6](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive)). For CHERI-TAL, instantiate foundational TAL/RustBelt/WasmCert-Coq soundness for the CHERI-RISC-V temporal-safety/typed-control-flow residual ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification), [§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization)); [typed-assembly-language.md](docs/languages/typed-assembly-language.md) owns its language.

  Build on Coq/Iris **Cerise** capability-machine universal contracts ([§13](docs/spec.md#13-packaging--supply-chain)), **Cerisier** attestation and **StkTokens** affine/linear stacks. **Katamaran** can discharge per-instruction separation contracts over μSail; R-13-016 still owes translation from the pinned Sail model.
- **Plan:** refine CIC against MetaCoq and CHERI-TAL against its soundness metatheorem to CompCert-C, then compile through CHERI-CompCert. Each package carries its hash-named exact source closure and correspondence through assembly, linking and image construction. Bring-up packages are reference components with thin/stubbed proof objects and derivations until Tier-0/1/2 proofs arrive; real evidence hardens their role in place.

  **Checker binaries are the bootstrap root** ([§6](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive), item 6). They rely on reproducible build, DDC and RoT measured boot; no package certificate admits the admitters themselves.

---

## 10. Emulating the hardware: reference execution and RTL comparison

Sail-C is reference and daily driver. S4 measures throughput; M7.1 composed boot against budget. M2 retains the retired QEMU fork and measured reopening condition; this section commissions no second emulator or general whole-machine harness.

- **Logic:** prove/check Gallina with generated campaigns. Reuse executable component oracles, staging new ones only at their consuming tasks; host logic needs no target compiler.
- **Target:** compile GC-free through the accepted purecap backend and M1.4′ assembler/composer, then run on Sail. M3.5 owns measured boot, M4.4 one kernel, M5.3 storage/block device, and M7.1 their userland-spine composition. Implement the roster's devices against reviewed contracts; address-map declarations alone implement none.
- **RTL:** R2 connects curated scalar RTL to RVFI-DII; R3 consumes M7.1's real image and console/event digests. Author trace adapter/image loader alongside core/software, joining real producer outputs at integration.
- **Tests/evidence:** the [differential corpus/trace schema](docs/assurance/differential-corpus.md) owns membership and record meanings. Reuse `run.py model corpus`, `run.py testrig`, `oracle` and `seed`; generate oracle-backed cases and retain minimized disagreements. Component, target and RTL checks share inputs without sharing verdicts.
- **Staging:** current Sail compositions have one hart; M4.4 accepts one instance, R2 multi-instance checks. Two-class memory/M0.16 sequencing are modeled interfaces, with physical evidence at R5/M9a. Scalar bring-up precedes M10 V/M/FEC and their measured-need gates. Neither multicore Sail harness nor deferred applications block M8a.

---

## 11. Building an FPGA from it all

The Sail golden model needs RTL for FPGA execution. The [two-language/semantic-anchor budget](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification) supports three routes ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)): formal-HDL refinement for new blocks, Sail-SV/commercial FEV for imported cores, and differential bring-up before closing Coq refinement ([§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization)).

- **(a) Author new blocks in Kôika/Kami.** Use Coq hardware DSLs for capability/tag DMA fabric, TDM NoC and fixed-function sequencers, proving refinement to Sail. PipelineGen and Sail → Kôika/Kami generation are scaffolding, not the design of record. Erbsen et al. (PLDI 2021, Bedrock2 + Kami) supplies an end-to-end software/hardware example. Each local theorem must name its endpoint and bind exact emitted SystemVerilog/synthesis inputs; an unverified emitter needs checked correspondence or an explicit evidence-tier seam. Q22d qualifies this boundary before M10 claims refinement.

  The interaction-tree line offers a second reference: Rocq RISC-V semantics with compiler-IR equivalence and a Kôika ALU validated against R-type integer operations, joined by bisimulation/refinement. Its three case studies supply no end-to-end theorem and change neither the closing proof nor licence choice.

  **Kami (`MIT`) is the default.** Kôika carries bare LGPL-2.1 terms without a per-file `LGPL-2.1-only`/`LGPL-2.1-or-later` election. Coq library imports may attach reciprocity to authored design, so a per-block Kôika election must identify the property Kami cannot supply, accept the stricter current LGPL-2.1 reading, and separate library-importing code from other code. Without all three, use Kami. This decision is due when route (a) opens, after co-simulation.
- **(b) Qualify imported cores with Sail-SV + commercial FEV.** Compare CHERI-CVA6 and Ara/Gemmini RTL to Sail's emitted SystemVerilog using a Jasper-class tool, scaling the CHERIoT-Ibex conformance method to application cores. This gives unbounded observational-equivalence evidence while trusting the FEV tool ([§6](docs/spec.md#6-trusted-computing-base-exhaustive), [§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)). Defer Coq closure to rebuilt Kôika blocks or a proof over shipped SystemVerilog when foundational Verilog semantics mature.
- **(c) Bring up open cores through differentials.** Use [Sail execution](#10-emulating-the-hardware-reference-execution-and-rtl-comparison) as the oracle for the early FPGA milestone. It requires agreement, without an RTL ⊑ Sail proof. Route (b) later strengthens imported-core evidence; route (a) supplies proven new blocks.

**Open RTL substrates** for route (b), initially exercised through (c), follow [§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization):

- **C-class:** CHERI-CVA6 (Bristol/lowRISC/OpenHW) or Bluespec CHERI-Flute/Piccolo/Toooba (CTSRD), modified for static-only prediction and a TSO store buffer ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)).
- **V/M/FEC:** PULP Ara, Berkeley Chisel Gemmini and open LDPC/polar cores.
- **RoT:** Ibex/OpenTitan functional references and CHERIoT-Ibex (Sonata) as CHERI-on-Ibex prior art; author scalar RV64+CHERI in the [main-die purecap format](#1-the-processor-the-fusion-soc-sail).

These are synthesizable SystemVerilog/Chisel substrates. lowRISC/Capabilities Limited's **COSMIC** (2025–28) hardens CHERI-CVA6 with OpenTitan IP and targets instruction/ISA conformance, providing application-class evidence toward the [realization constraint](docs/spec.md#18-realization); [inspirations.md](docs/background/inspirations.md) records its scope.

**CHERI Mocha**, COSMIC MVP-2 (`lowRISC/mocha`, pinned `v0.1.1`), supplies an FPGA-synthesizable CVA6-CHERI/OpenTitan secure-enclave SoC booting to a terminal on Genesys 2 (Kintex-7).

- **Edition:** `v0.0.1` is MVP-1 bare-metal CHERI/integer testing; `v0.1.0` is integrated MVP-2 with CHERI-Linux; pinned `v0.1.1` adds an FPGA reset fix without changing blocks. Untagged default-branch work is newer. December 2026 RC-1 is uncut; the announcement describes the initial release and the README availability table marks only `0.0.1` available.
- **Reusable references:** application datapath; entropy source, KMAC, ROM controller, SPI host and mailbox for RoT/boot; KMAC for SHAKE-heavy PQ crypto; and `sw/device/lib/hal` for verified DMA/MMIO/descriptors.
- **Tag fabric:** the AXI controller carries one tag per 128-bit bus region on user bits, with separate tag memory. Adapt this shape to R-15-203's 64-bit granule, rather than copying parameters. It models the single validity plane; Write-before-Read is a type attribute ([§5](docs/spec.md#5-languages--verification)). This addresses the owed capability/tag fabric ([§17](docs/spec.md#17-residual-risks-the-honest-ceiling)).
- **Counters/keys:** OTP rollback ports inform the [monotonic floor](docs/spec.md#9-boot--root-of-trust). Leave memory-encryption-key ports unused: [§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform) excludes memory-path keys, cipher and integrity construction, reducing new RTL.
- **Assurance:** retain functional-reference status under route (b). Current staged OpenTitan-style sign-off uses UVM, bounded FPV and three-reviewer checklists, with first sign-offs targeted at RC-1 (December 2026); this supplies no unbounded RTL ⊑ ISA proof ([§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization)).
- **Exclusions:** development debug is gated by RoT lifecycle and fused off in production ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)); after deleting `Zicntr`/`Zihpm`, its trace is the sole development cycle-measurement path. Do not adopt CHERI-Linux or a Linux-personality shim ([§14](docs/spec.md#14-userland)). Mocha chooses an application core for enclave MMU support, whereas this profile deletes the MMU (`satp` Bare, [§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform)); curate that path away, without transferring its conformance evidence to hardware outside its scope.

**Purecap staging.** Existing CHERI RV64 soft cores and the [functional compiler prerequisite](#0-the-discipline-two-languages-two-golden-models) let C-class FPGA bring-up boot the purecap golden-model images from the start. The new [RTL/Sail work](docs/spec.md#18-realization) is capability-aware Ara/Gemmini vector/matrix memory operations with per-element checks. CHERI-RVV supplies design prior art: capability references for all vector memory instructions, one amortized bounds check for elements inside a known range and unchanged legacy vector code compiling purecap. It is an ISAv8 proposal, not a frozen-dialect implementation of either core. Amortized versus per-element checks remain a magnitude question for R-15-085a's vector-memory rows and R-17-041. Fabricating a shipping CHERI ASIC is a later [silicon gate](docs/spec.md#18-realization), separate from emulation/FPGA.

**RTL profile curation.** The [frozen profile](docs/hardware/isa-profile.md) owns required behavior; the [absence contract](docs/hardware/absence-contract.md) owns forbidden structures and elaborated-netlist evidence. Record state enumeration and synthesis-configuration provenance at first elaboration, when disabling parameters are chosen. Most curation removes hardware. Capability width instead requires datapath re-parameterization: imported ISAv8/v9 cores use 128-bit capabilities, while the [frozen format](docs/hardware/isa-profile.md#41-the-capability-format) is 64+1 bits. Sail owns that format; generated vectors and the existing trace rig check RTL directly, without an intervening QEMU implementation.

**M8b's co-simulation artifact** ([§12](#12-build-order-milestones-and-execution-state)) is a versioned, lint-clean, elaborated SoC top: curated scalar C-class, tag fabric and R1c-ii's composed-image devices, authored sources/pinned imports, synthesis provenance and first-elaboration absence evidence. Under Verilator, its capability-widened `rvfi` trace must agree with Sail over the shared corpus, using CVA6's `rvfi`/tracer port, and M7.1's purecap image must boot with matching console/event digests ([§10](#10-emulating-the-hardware-reference-execution-and-rtl-comparison)). Guest tooling supplies these checks; Host CI does not. Later board synthesis/execution checks fit, timing closure and the same corpus.

Co-simulation exercises **both static memory latencies** and bank-staggered maintenance ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform), R-15-247): bulk accesses return at the second constant; mode transitions use the [§12](docs/spec.md#12-system-servers-contained-outside-the-tcb) discharge/refresh sequencer, reading fail-stop completion once after fixed dwell. The FPGA has no oxide deck, so it models these constants. R5 must measure them on a repaired macro under R-15-247m; this gate establishes interface/sequencing evidence, not physical-device qualification.

**Synthesis order:** synthesize the artifact that passed co-simulation, targeting a large UltraScale+/Versal-class FPGA or an open board with sufficient capacity. Bring up **C scalar → V → M → FEC → NoC → islands**, differentially checking each class against Sail; scalar boots the same [firmware/kernel/init](#10-emulating-the-hardware-reference-execution-and-rtl-comparison). The authored RoT follows R1c's route-(a) decision and separate hardening scope, outside the M8b roster. Additional classes require reviewed entry conditions.

**Deferred hardening:** R2's riscv-formal/rvfi BMC smoke is required before co-simulation acceptance. Imported-core Sail-SV/commercial FEV, Isla obligations and authored-block Kami/Kôika RTL ⊑ Sail proofs follow the FPGA milestone, still targeting Sail ([§15](docs/spec.md#15-hardware-platform), [§18](docs/spec.md#18-realization)).

---

## 12. Build order, milestones, and execution state

Milestone numbers group deliverables and evidence. Authoring starts with its named
inputs and acceptance predicate; integration waits for its consumed artifacts.
Software (M) and RTL (R) close at **M8a** and **M8b**. Shared decisions/instruments
(S) and assessment actions (Q) gate only their explicit consumers. The earliest
Q items arose from [the critique's product assessment](docs/background/critique.md#the-two-theses);
later ones compare register obligations with external evidence.

Realization is **scalar-first**, with V/M/FEC datapaths last (M10), admitted on
measured need. Capability width instead follows R-15-007d's final frozen profile
for immutable-image capabilities and sealed blobs. R-15-007's conditional RVY
review may amend width/format before freeze; a later format break requires an
approved transition covering stored-authority invalidation or reprovisioning.

A crown-jewel row entering `authored` immediately enters R-05-150's independent
specification review at the edition its proofs will use.

**Read an upstream's own license before incorporation at its milestone.** Pinning
for inspection differs from vendoring or translating artifacts, whose terms bind
the result. [THIRD-PARTY.md](THIRD-PARTY.md) records incorporation status;
[COPYRIGHT.md](COPYRIGHT.md) owns this tree's licenses. Each incorporating item
names its terms. Non-permissive inputs have decisions at M1.1a (compiler), M6.0a
(control-plane compiler) and M4.1a (shipped kernel specification); M2.1 instead
contains the development-only emulator fork. Discovering incompatible terms
later can require discarding the work.

Pinned Wasm guest semantics (inventory row 25, R-14-013b) is a first-release
same-session app-host prerequisite. WasmCert/Iris-Wasm's
[qualified scope](docs/assurance/proof-reuse/parsers.md#wasmcert-coq-type-safety-and-interpreter-refinement-with-concrete-boundaries)
still requires binary parsing, concrete numeric/SIMD and array operations,
dependency compatibility and local host-confinement correspondence. Q34g prices
U-15's curation, U-16's pinned artifact, the interpreter and both theorems; selecting
sources or reviewing a subset closes none. M1's host-side CertiCoq → Wasm oracle
is separate build-farm machinery and leaves R-05-085's device claim untouched.

The [crown-jewel inventory](docs/assurance/crown-jewels.md) also has `not authored`
rows with no authoring item: NoC/island isolation (8), bank/island binding (9),
wire formats/Narcissus descriptors (10), OPP/mode schedules (11), reset/power
sequences (12), calibration-manifest schema (13), timing-model latency magnitudes
(15) and verified HAL hardware contracts (18). Constraint references in other
items do not retire them; each needs register-based scope and pricing.
**Q3c owns protected-sequence fault-model authoring (24)**, which remains
`not authored` until the artifact and independent review exist. Detection theorems
and countermeasure construction remain outside that estimate, as recorded after
M10. Security-policy row 2 has candidates in
[security-policy-model.md](docs/assurance/security-policy-model.md) and
[SecurityPolicyModel.v](proofs/SecurityPolicyModel.v); runtime timing release,
concrete composition/strategy bindings and independent review remain open,
including the scope consumed by CJ-SECOMP and B-07/P-1.

**R-15-108's DSE is deferred in [§1](#1-the-processor-the-fusion-soc-sail) and
unpriced.** Its pending outputs include M0.8a's `frozen` flag, M0.8c's scratchpad
size, M0.13's six rows, M0.15's comparison arm, M0.17's six symbols, M6.8's bank
grant and R1c's per-class pipeline depth. Like route (a)'s closing refinement,
it lies outside the grand total; `frozen` and `qualified` flags do not close it.

Radio reference rows 19–22 are also outside this milestone clock: 802.11 MLME
(row 21) belongs to the first-release roster (R-18-004); rows 19, 20 and 22 belong
to R-02-003b's handset. R-12-043e requires an exact licensed symbolic artifact and
formal-to-formal review. The [protocol inventory](docs/assurance/proof-reuse/protocols.md)
covers slices: AKA/NAS, handover/RRC, WPA2–SAE/MLME and pairing/L2CAP–GATT differ
in scope. Each complete row still needs variant selection, missing procedures
and timers, transcription and implementation correspondence. The
[unassigned proof map](docs/assurance/unassigned-proof-map.md) proposes U-17 source
qualification and U-18's first Bluetooth pairing subset; remaining procedures,
other models and sequencer refinements need separate scope.

### Checklist conventions

* **Name the accepted artifact.** Completed titles and summaries distinguish specifications, statements, surveys and calculations from implementations and measurements. The latter require executable or measured evidence at their stated boundary. A landed specification names the open implementation owner and predicate; compilation and closed proof assumptions alone do not close it.
* **Keep landed entries short.** Retain the header, estimate cell, one figure-free result line and a link to [the completion log](docs/implementation/completion-log.md). Open notes stay here. Each landed or struck item has one log heading with its exact label, accepted result, decisive validation/provenance, material limits, findings and landing tier. Omit dispatch plans, progress histories, transcripts, diff sizes, routine gate inventories and incidental counts. K-82 checks item/log correspondence and findings; K-84 checks landing declarations; K-113 checks the summary and own-entry link; K-12 resolves it.
* **Report item-specific evidence.** State what the gate measured. Keep the standing profile-sweep figure (199 of 199 refusals from M0.6f) and legacy-rig prefix (67 records across all 54 `rv64ui-p-*` members) only where they change. A proof item reports its file's constant count, not the changing global total over [proofs/](proofs/).
* **One authored estimate cell per priced item.** Open: `· 6 h, range 4–8 · X`; completed: `· 1.9 h actual`. Use attended agent-session hours unless marked otherwise; re-price splits. The midpoint is the mean of range endpoints. Parents whose children carry estimates have none; cells carry no share of the grand total.
* **Irrecoverable historical timing may retain the last estimate.** Write `· 6 h retained estimate, actual n/a`; preserve the original range, accepted evidence and missing intervals in the log. This is a planning weight, not measured effort; a current review/build cannot replace missing work. Completion requires Tier A and unchanged technical gates. Both calibration columns read `n/a` (K-96 rejects a measured pair); summaries separately derive retained weights and item counts. Completed scope combines actuals and retained weights, not cumulative actual time.
* **Generate all totals, ranges and progress figures from cells** with `tools/check.py --fix`. Full priced scope includes conditional work and unfunded options. [work-order.json](docs/implementation/work-order.json) assigns every open priced leaf one disposition; new or ambiguous leaves are findings, not implicit gate commitments.
* **Price proof reuse against the remaining local obligation.** The [proof inventory](docs/assurance/proof-reuse.md) records dated source/license readings, theorem locations and transfer gaps. Cells name reused sources, local qualification and remaining acquisition, adaptation, representation, assumption, implementation and integration work. Existing start-froms receive no second discount; replacing a completed helper changes no actual. Ranges are engineering judgments, not survey-measured productivity. Conditional implementation starts at its checked prerequisites; missing mandatory foundations get owners and separate estimates first.
* **Class every open estimate:** `I` for repository authority, `X` for external authority. The [schedule basis](#estimate-and-schedule-basis) owns calibration; X uses the attended `X-authored` pool. Calibration records retain historical pools/earliest estimates; cells own actuals. K-86 requires an X upper endpoint at least twice its lower; I has no span floor. Calibration never re-prices midpoints.
* **Keep execution clocks separate.** `agent-parallel` marks fan-out; its actual sums agent-session wall time across authoring, verification and repair. Attended actuals sum attended intervals. Their fits have no measured conversion and are never pooled. Unmeasured fan-out items retain the label but supply no actual to [the parallel calibration](#the-agent-parallel-series). Execution mode changes neither gates nor tier.
* **Workers return focused evidence; the integrator validates the stable batch.** Follow [check scheduling](tools/README.md#check-scheduling-during-fan-out): batch document checks, target changed rules/behavior, report tested inputs, drift and deferred checks, and leave `--fix`, full selftests and bare `run.py` to integration. A red checker baseline postpones selftesting and supplies no mutation verdict. The integrator settles inputs, repairs derived facts and completes the required hosted wave. Handoffs remain provisional until every landing condition holds, including the full mutation verdict where required.
* **Make figures reproducible.** Counts name their counting predicate; figures name their revision. Different patterns can yield different counts on identical bytes, and a moving checkout cannot identify evidence. F-208 records this finding class.
* **`Wasm-parallel` is a legacy completion label.** Gallina authoring needs its reviewed contract and proof environment; use `run.py proofs` and applicable generated campaigns. Target compilation/image staging belongs to the consumer. Use the Wasm oracle only where the comparison requires it; M1.5 demonstrates the loop and M4.3 its staged IPC client.
* **Use content-validated incremental proof acceptance.** For changed proof inputs, `python tools/run.py proofs` covers the current corpus, reusing validated unchanged kernel verdicts. Completion alone does not require `proofs --fresh`. Share identical-input evidence while retaining item-specific semantic reviews/campaigns. The [proof evidence contract](tools/README.md#current-evidence-and-generated-documentation) owns invalidation/full rechecks; `proofs status` checks source/output freshness without toolchain reprobes. Run proof acceptance through the hosted validation handoff.
* **Landing has two tiers.** Tier A requires a full attended read for register, profile, crown-jewel, absence-contract or coverage edits; moved co-read pairs; new normative claims; and decisions. Tier B spot-reads findings only when the checker is green with every mutant killed, no Tier-A edit occurred, the co-read ledger is unchanged, and a rule holds each created fact.
* **Declare the landing tier in the log:** one `Landed: Tier A` or `Landed: Tier B under` line. Tier B names in bold every rule holding a created fact. Bold rule IDs are holder citations; plain IDs are discussion. Existing holders qualify when extended. K-84 checks citations against [the registry](tools/check-rules.md), allowing retired rules only in historical log evidence, and requires a Tier-B holder. It checks declarations where made, without assigning missing historical tiers. Review still decides whether each cited rule holds the claimed fact.
* **Land the acceptance predicate before implementation starts.** New items carry it at entry; an unwritable predicate records its refusal and missing input in the same cell. This enables self-checking and Tier B; [S18's log](docs/implementation/completion-log.md#s18-author-the-acceptance-predicates-five-open-items-owe) records the initial application.
* **Generate validation wherever an oracle exists.** M2.1 demonstrated 21,546 vectors; R1a demonstrated 658,659 with twelve seeded defects killed. A hand-written corpus member where a generator suffices is a finding.
* **Give each fact one owner; generate every restatement from it.** Re-emit from the model, register or contract during checking. A hand-maintained duplicate merely compared by a rule is a finding.
* **Separate authoring inputs from integration joins.** Each dispatched leaf's note names **Start** (exact contract/artifact), **Owns** (deliverable/write boundary), **Check** (predicate/focused command), **Join** (required producer outputs) and **Execute** (reuse, work split or early rejection). Point to existing cell detail rather than repeating it; [Sequencing](#sequencing) supplies the shared procedure. A parent may allocate work but cannot replace a leaf's note: M1.2's allocation does not supply M1.2e's representation/slot-plan start. Later milestones remain deliverable-level until entry. Missing leaf notes are gaps, currently judged by reading rather than a label-checking rule. Missing contracts/predicates block affected implementation only; review and independent siblings may proceed. Fixtures permit local checks but cannot replace real producers at joins.
* **Split by independently checkable output.** One owner lands shared types, schemas or calling conventions before consumers change them in separate modules. Work packages may split an existing estimate, but the parent stays open through every acceptance clause. Independent completion credit requires re-priced child cells and no parent estimate. Concurrency alone changes no estimate or evidence.
* **`after the M8a gate` defers priority unless an input exists only at that gate.** Spare capacity may prepare independent contracts, host logic and harnesses once their start conditions hold. Delivery/roster membership, scope-cut proofs and scalar-first/measured-need datapath rules stay fixed. Final freezes and measurements require their complete corpus and inputs.
* **Every fan-out subagent uses a dedicated Git worktree**, including scouts, reviewers, document workers and nested agents. Before dispatch, create a fresh repository lane or verify a dedicated host-provisioned checkout at the intended revision. The brief names absolute path, branch/detached HEAD, base, ownership, checks and integrator; every call targets that checkout. Only the integrator writes integration. Follow [the isolation procedure](tools/README.md#worktree-isolation-during-fan-out) for placement/lifecycle.
* **`Parallel` follows the item's dispatch conditions, independent of document order.** Its worktree owns its build outputs. Lanes isolate files, build/iterate trees, SMT caches, logs and emulators ([I7](#build-loop-instruments)); a second build in the same lane is refused, while sibling lanes may build concurrently. They still share one twelve-core WSL machine: reserve CPU, review and mutable-toolchain capacity. Document/provenance/checker work need not build the model.
* **One integrator owns shared files, review and gate scheduling.** Workers return commit/input identities, focused evidence, findings, deferred checks and proposed completion notes. The integrator reconciles checklist, log, arithmetic and shared registrations. **Check** distinguishes lane commands from remaining integration gates. Reserve Tier-A review, prioritize consumer-releasing contracts and M8a repairs, stabilize the batch through validation and reuse unchanged-input evidence. Overlapping edits and shared installs require coordination. `run.py seed properties` owns its checkout exclusively against all readers, writers, gates and staging for its entire mutation run; use isolated checkouts for other work.

### Current summary

M4.4b-i and M7.1e have passing joint target evidence for the finite scalar composition: normal restart, stale-grant refusal and incomplete-completion refusal. The completion log binds the final receipt to the accepted source and model inputs; full roster boot remains open.

These figures cover the priced reference-build, assessment and named hardening backlog, including unfunded options. They neither commission every option nor fully cost the end-to-end proof program: compiler/security preservation, native refinement/admission, binary constant-time, WCET, storage crash/concurrency/liveness composition and RTL-to-Sail proofs remain incompletely priced. Fabrication, laboratory calendar and additional qualification campaigns also lie outside these cells; existing hardware items retain their measured-device/laboratory boundaries. [Proof proposals](#proof-and-specification-proposals) and [mandatory unpriced work](#mandatory-work-outside-the-priced-total), traced through [the proof map](docs/assurance/unassigned-proof-map.md), enter the sums only when priced. Missing prices never imply zero. Actuals, retained weights and execution clocks follow the conventions above.

* Item checkboxes below own completion status; [Sequencing](#sequencing) owns the work order, and [the completion log](docs/implementation/completion-log.md) owns landing evidence.

Item cells and [calibration records](#calibration-record) generate this summary. Completed scope includes retained estimates with unavailable actuals. The calibrated total uses [attended authority-pool ratios](#estimate-and-schedule-basis), keeping execution clocks separate. **Committed remaining** sums open M8a, M8b and other committed leaves; **Remaining** also includes conditional work and unfunded options. Reclassifying optional work changes commitment, not full backlog effort. Q34j holds optional Wasm work after the minimal workflow checkpoint; unselected optimizations remain uncommissioned.

* **Committed M8a** names only the scalar software roster's required leaves. **Committed M8b** names the parallel RTL deliverables; it consumes the M7 image without acquiring the rest of the software review. **Other committed** covers the remaining priced release and hardening obligations, with their existing delivery boundaries.
* **Conditional** retains a priced trial or connection whose selected input, prerequisite qualification or adoption decision remains open. It does not cancel the required property. **Unfunded option** retains an estimate for an uncommissioned branch; reopening requires its stated admission decision. Deferral changes current priority, not total engineering effort.
* [work-order.json](docs/implementation/work-order.json) owns these explicit, disjoint leaf sets. K-96 refuses an unclassified, multiply assigned, missing or ambiguous leaf. Completed leaves may remain in the owner for dispatch references but contribute no open hours. No broad parent sum or division by agent count is an elapsed critical-path forecast.

### Sequencing

Dispatch by item-local **Start** and **Join** conditions. Prioritize contracts and repairs releasing the compiler-to-boot path. Open independent lanes with clear write boundaries, useful outputs and reserved review/build capacity; integrate queued work before adding more.

**Qualify the producer before final target capture.** Component authoring and focused runs continue during Q39's compiler diagnosis/qualification. Final shared M7.1 capture, re-staged manifests and M8a acceptance require that qualified identity; demonstrate Q43's unchanged reuse before repeating downstream builds. Interrupted audits supply no accepted producer. Q39's unresolved diagnosis and cold audit runtime remain outside its conditional attended estimate.

**Screen physical supply before broad laptop-specific implementation.** Bring Q6a's bounded supply inquiry forward using Q4a/Q5a's existing demand and placement records and R4's measurands. Q34f/g/h and Q41a/b may prepare their contracts and independent interfaces, but broad implementation opens only after Q6a records a named credible process/macro supply path for the retained release workload and guarantees. Literature density, absent coefficients or a simulated constant cannot satisfy that decision. The existing scalar M8a/M8b work continues; an adverse supply verdict returns to Q10's product decision and adopts no smaller workload, external-memory interface or weaker physical claim.

**Qualify one real proof consumer before expanding the route.** Close Q35d's two actual cold-path functions with Q35b's strictly compiled canonical term and reviewed machine-logic increments before extending across the kernel. Each shared foundation needs an implementation client, positive/refusal cases and its contracted exact assumption/kernel evidence. Return failed interfaces or missing premises to their owner before dependent expansion; new frameworks and unpriced ports require commissioning. Independent M8a authoring continues. U-23/U-24's memory-sequencer hardware proof remains a separately priced proposal until its source and contract are commissioned.

The generated view reports M8a leaf joins from [work-order.json](docs/implementation/work-order.json). Authoring requires completed prerequisite leaves, existing artifact files and no explicit unmet predicate. This permits work against the reviewed contract without certifying implementation readiness, producer acceptance or a full gate. Acceptance joins name exact outputs and disappear only on producer completion; `n/a` still owes the leaf's own predicate. Mutual joins may be tested/accepted together without awaiting whole sibling completion. This is a dispatch/accounting view, not a topological schedule. External conditions remain unmet until deciding evidence is reviewed; the supervisor's capped lowering-route trial has its own prerequisite leaf.

Modeled constants suffice only for the co-simulation claims |
