## Static-memory research

This research backlog adds no implementation schedule, landing credit or accepted requirement. Selected work remains in the [project plan](PLAN.md): Q5 owns placement comparisons, Q4 inference demand, Q6 topology, Q8 measured bottlenecks, Q22 assurance boundaries and Q10 product judgment. Reuse their artifacts and return findings to those owners.

The [replayable first experiments](docs/implementation/static-memory/experiments.md) provide a mathematical baseline, synthetic corpus, diagnostic ledger and bounded placement comparison. Status applies only to the stated deliverable: a reviewed baseline or witness leaves its implementation, product, proof and generality obligations open.

The [worked replay walkthrough](docs/implementation/static-memory/experiments.md#walkthrough-three-kinds-of-unavailable-capacity) diagnoses owner isolation, separated free extents and delayed device completion; receipts distinguish free backing, usable declared slots and completed reuse.

- **Status: complete.** **Define the mathematical and operational baseline.** Produce a reviewed model of payload liveness, retained authority, safe reuse, legal placement, and per-arena physical charge. Reconcile R-08-011 through R-08-013 with the exact theorem assumptions and the placement-search quantities. Deliver a proof or minimal counterexample for each disputed implication before optimizing it.
  Completion evidence: the [reviewed disposition](docs/implementation/static-memory/baseline.md#baseline-research-disposition) proves or refutes every disputed implication. The [operational interface](docs/implementation/static-memory/baseline.md#the-operational-interface-a-producer-must-satisfy) states binding, interference and completed-reuse premises and proves conditional isolation/occupancy bounds. R-08-011 through R-08-014 distinguish these premises from ownership, state the charged-load inequality and scoped laminar theorem, and correct the interference predicate. The [mode comparison](docs/implementation/static-memory/modes.md) separates per-mode optima from a common layout. Sound admitted-program intervals and the qualified reuse gate remain open.
  The [mode-family witnesses](docs/implementation/static-memory/modes.md#the-declared-witnesses) exercise live source/destination state: a live destination refuses the binding family even with a dead source, without a binding charge or optimality claim.
- **Status: open.** **Build a reproducible capacity corpus.** Start from Q5's exporter and the real composed roster when available. Include bounded parsers, sessions, rings, saved application state, frame pipelines and inference, plus synthetic crossing lifetimes, adversarial sizes, burst teardown and delayed device completion. Record source revision, manifest, all cost assumptions and demand envelope. Keep witness demonstrations labeled as such.
  Current output: the [corpus and Q5 bridge](docs/implementation/static-memory/corpus.md) identify synthetic contracts for every family, assumptions, demand series and computed coverage. Witnesses distinguish total free bytes, contiguous geometry and declared slot size; a same-owner size-class witness refuses a larger request despite contiguous idle backing, then fits its declared slot at completed reuse. The bridge reports missing source fields; actual-roster contracts with per-component extents remain absent.
  The [alignment-stranding witness](docs/implementation/static-memory/corpus.md#geometric-fit-and-typed-refusals) changes refusal to instantaneous fit by relaxing request alignment alone. `same-owner-arena-stranding` fits an idle slot in another arena while the requested arena waits for its own completed reuse; neither permits cross-arena capacity transfer.

  - **Status: complete.** Build and replay the [synthetic capacity corpus and Q5 bridge](docs/implementation/static-memory/corpus.md#synthetic-corpus-disposition), covering every named family with source and manifest identities, explicit cost assumptions, bounded demand traces and witness labels. Preserve Q5's missing operational inputs; real-roster contracts remain open.

- **Status: open.** **Implement a diagnostic byte ledger.** For each owner, arena and admitted mode, report useful payload, reserved span, disjoint overhead charges, occupancy, unreusable retired bytes, largest legally usable extent and typed refusals. Preserve telemetry labels. Derive every figure from artifacts; measure equivalent service and alternative service contracts separately.
  Current output: the [event ledger](docs/implementation/static-memory/corpus.md) partitions bytes and diagnoses bounded requests under explicit witness premises. Target telemetry and compiled-service cost extraction remain open.

  - **Status: complete.** Validate the [finite-trace byte ledger and request diagnostics](docs/implementation/static-memory/corpus.md#finite-trace-ledger-disposition): independently check disjoint charges at every fixture tick, distinguish free geometry from idle declared slots, and preserve source identity, telemetry, cost, reuse and service-contract labels on every fit and typed refusal.

- **Status: complete.** **Establish exact small-instance oracles.** Exhaustively enumerate small layouts and use independently checked lower bounds to separate unavoidable fragmentation from planner error. Mutate lifetimes, alignment, owner assignment, slot overlap and reuse barriers; invalid candidates must fail. A feasible certificate and an optimality certificate are different deliverables.
  Completion evidence: the [exact oracle and independent replay](docs/implementation/static-memory/experiments.md), [representability-granule layer](docs/implementation/static-memory/representability.md) and [span lower bounds](docs/implementation/static-memory/scaling.md) distinguish feasibility from finite-model optimality. The [mutation sweep](docs/implementation/static-memory/mutants.md), independent byte/tick checks and refusal controls reject placement defects, premature reuse at every completion barrier and internally consistent feasible-but-suboptimal certificates. Incomplete searches and stillborn mutants supply no successful verdict. Remaining capability-format rules and actual authority-completion extraction are outside this oracle.
- **Status: open.** **Scale offline planning.** Benchmark deterministic heuristics and coupled search against the existing enumeration and exact oracles. Report best feasible span, proved lower bound, remaining gap, build time and reproducibility. Preserve the standing plan on timeout or failure to find improvement. Introduce and license-review dependencies only when that comparison justifies them.
  Current output: the [scaling experiment](docs/implementation/static-memory/scaling.md) compares checked heuristics, bounded exact search and Q5's witness, reporting each arena's strongest lower bound, checked span, gap and work completeness. Interrupted lowering retains partial improvements diagnostically while selecting the standing plan. Coupled-search benchmarking remains open; actual-roster and target constraints remain Q5b work.

  - **Status: complete.** Establish [reproducible synthetic heuristic benchmarks](docs/implementation/static-memory/scaling.md#replay-and-receipt) with exact-oracle and Q5 comparisons, per-arena spans, lower bounds and gaps, identified host timings, and standing-plan preservation under work-budget exhaustion. Coupled-search comparison, actual-roster constraints and target build measurements remain open.

- **Status: open.** **Prototype lifetime and representation transformations.** Compare lexical regions, explicit bounded phases and selected non-lexical release; compile one service both contiguously and into fixed bounded chunks. Prove result equivalence and account for aliases, capability narrowing, descriptors, vectorization and DMA staging. Record whether each gain is due to less live data or better placement.
  Current output: the [frame-service experiment](docs/implementation/static-memory/transformations.md) compares retention, early release, chunks and rematerialization, with modeled descriptor and staging charges. [StaticMemoryService.v](proofs/StaticMemoryService.v) proves the list-functional models equivalent under stated partition premises. Refinement of the emitted programs, authority and DMA behavior remains open.

  - **Status: complete.** Complete the [finite frame-service transformation prototype](docs/implementation/static-memory/transformations.md#finite-prototype-disposition): compare retention, phases, early release and bounded chunks under one configured service, with independent output, erasure, bounds, placement and disjoint-byte checks. Emitted-program refinement and target alias, narrowing, vectorization and DMA obligations remain open.

- **Status: open.** **Explore the bytes-versus-work frontier.** Generate static rematerialization, tiling and fusion variants. Derive worst-case execution and traffic from the emitted code, including zeroization and code-size growth. A candidate closes only if the same service fits its original deadlines, memory classes, power constraints and admitted image.
  Current output: the [transformation frontier](docs/implementation/static-memory/transformations.md) counts abstract work and traffic, and the [compiled census](docs/implementation/static-memory/census.md) derives two kernels through Rupicola and a contained compiler. Its plain-RV64 instruction counts are a proxy; target WCET, image size, power and composed deadlines remain unqualified.
- **Status: open.** **Co-design reclamation and capacity.** Derive worst-case retirement envelopes and complete release-to-reuse bounds. Exercise saved-register retention, grant loans, stalled endpoints, restart storms and stale capabilities behind the sweep cursor. Compare quarantine bytes and service availability across fixed sweep schedules, using Q22's boundary obligations.
  Current output: the [reclamation experiment](docs/implementation/static-memory/reclamation.md) derives calendar terms from Q22a holder signatures and compares fixed schedules. The [adversarial envelope](docs/implementation/static-memory/envelope.md) supplies declared retirement budgets, release-to-reuse bounds and bounded search checks. Actual holder coverage, admitted deadlines and qualified service rates remain open.
  The [reclamation walkthrough](docs/implementation/static-memory/reclamation.md#receipt-walkthrough) follows the generated receipt from the baseline and joint schedules to traffic refusals and failed reuse. It distinguishes recovered backing from peak quarantine and shows why failed slots remain charged.

  - **Status: complete.** Validate the [finite reclamation calendar and adversarial envelope](docs/implementation/static-memory/reclamation.md#finite-reclamation-research-disposition), with declared retirement budgets, fixed-schedule capacity comparisons, conditional reuse bounds, independent byte/tick checks and Q22a refusal witnesses. Actual holder coverage, admitted workloads, target service rates and all-executions proofs remain open.

- **Status: open.** **Prove or refute the structural questions.** Formalize the laminar special case and the restricted-lifetime parameterization, then characterize which added constraints break them. Publish negative results and minimal witnesses as first-class outputs. A mechanized feasibility check does not close the algorithmic optimality problem.
  Current output: the [mechanized laminar statement](docs/implementation/static-memory/baseline.md#mechanized-statement) proves scoped placement and a crossing-family gap. The [deletion-parameter study](docs/implementation/static-memory/structure.md) computes the parameter polynomially, constructs bipartite crossing graphs and refutes decompositions. Source-lifetime bridging, general replay correspondence and parameterized complexity remain open; the narrower results below are complete.

  - **Status: complete.** Prove the [sharp triangle-free gap threshold](docs/implementation/static-memory/structure.md#a-sharp-gap-threshold-for-triangle-free-crossing-graphs): deletion number three permits a gap, and smaller deletion numbers cannot in that graph class. The unrestricted threshold is narrowed to `{2, 3}`.
  - **Status: complete.** Replay the explicit gap witness with independent feasibility and optimality checks, and establish inclusion-minimality by checking every proper subfamily. This does not establish the least object count over all possible witnesses.
  - **Status: complete.** Prove the [extent-gcd lattice lemma](docs/implementation/static-memory/structure.md#an-exact-search-without-address-enumeration) and use it in exact search, including common scaling, capacities between lattice points and incomplete-search checks. This is not a parameterized complexity result.

- **Status: open.** **Isolate one architecture experiment if justified.** Select public phase reuse, typed relocation, or capacity lending only after baseline measurements show the limitation it addresses. Keep an explicit changed-assumption ledger and prove the transition's authority, timing and information-flow claims. Reject a candidate with an unbounded pause, hidden reserve, or unreconciled exclusion.
  Current output: the [public-phase model](docs/implementation/static-memory/phases.md) tests compositional reuse certificates, while the [lending model](docs/implementation/static-memory/lending.md) separates refusal and timing channels with two-run witnesses. Both carry explicit changed assumptions and finite scope. Neither establishes a qualified product limitation or selects a new admission rule; cross-owner lending remains excluded.
- **Status: open.** **Run a composition-level comparison.** Feed promising variants into Q5b and the product gate on the same roster and functional contract. Report which additional workload is admitted, all resource deltas, exhaustion behavior and any weakened promise. Perform ablations so packing, lifetime changes, segmentation and reclamation do not each claim the same recovered bytes.
  Current output: the reclamation and transformation reports compare declared ablations, the compiled census supplies a proxy, and the scaling report retains the existing Q5 witness. M7.1's actual roster, compiled service contracts and qualified product resource inputs remain required before Q5b can credit additional admitted workload.
- **Status: open.** **Publish a reproducible research artifact and disposition.** Release definitions, proofs, generators, minimized counterexamples, checker inputs, search settings and replayable measurements. Identify peer-reviewed results, preprints, new conjectures and measured outcomes separately. Propose register changes only with the precise theorem or measured contract that replaces the current claim; an inconclusive experiment remains open.
  Current output: the [artifact inventory](docs/implementation/static-memory/artifact.md) derives files/actions, checks completeness and classifies results. Replay rejects malformed research and experiment receipts even after successful action exits: action identity must match and scope be nonempty, with contract-bearing research cases. Expected refusals and incomplete searches retain diagnostic scope. The [replay guide](docs/implementation/static-memory/experiments.md) binds sources and inputs. The CLI refuses search budgets on actions that do not consume them. External publication/review, remaining mechanized refinements and measured product comparison stay open.

  - **Status: complete.** Validate the [local research inventory and registered-action replay](docs/implementation/static-memory/artifact.md#local-artifact-disposition), including indexed sources, completeness, classification, document reachability and malformed-receipt rejection. Intentional refusals and incomplete searches retain their diagnostic scope; external publication, review, implementation refinements and product comparison remain open.

Research combines programming-language semantics, algorithms, real-time systems, capability security and compiler engineering. Success means a checked increase in useful admitted work with explicit costs; proving a reservation unavoidable or an optimization isolation-breaking is also a result.

## Coverage-preserving tooling retirement

Retire implementations only when replacements preserve complete input populations, observable decisions and refusals. Shared commands retain distinct acceptance obligations. Capability-vector consolidation adds no dependency, prover upgrade, performance claim or milestone credit.

- **Capability-vector generation. Status: implemented.** Typed probes use the shared oracle emitter for formatting, random generation and printing; domain helpers and ordered sweeps remain Sail input. The monolithic generator is retired. Focused controls/typecheck pass. The [retained hosted comparison](tools/cheri-equiv/README.md#migration-evidence) establishes identical complete ordered vectors against the immutable predecessor, including exhaustive domains and corners; it retains the retired qualification workflow/helper. `retire-tools-20261030-final` owns final Host CI and all Guest dispatches. The independent RTL consumer retains hosted acceptance; no speedup is claimed.
- **Concurrent link-validator installation. Status: implemented.** Reviewed FileLock serializes per-environment installation/cache revalidation. Stable lock identity and its required native backend preserve producer-lock exclusion. Archive/executable pins, atomic publication and corrupt-cache refusals remain. Independent-process controls cover one cold install, failure/crash recovery, bounded refusal and corruption; focused Windows controls/typecheck pass. Ubuntu Host CI tests FileLock/flock contention both ways. The final journal owns both platform verdicts and all Guest dispatches.
- **Rocq context workaround. Status: conditional. Start:** a qualified prover-lock
  move carrying the merged checker fix described by the
  [opam adoption contract](tools/opam/README.md#merged-checker-and-printer-improvements).
  **Owns:** comparison of native admitted-library context output with the current
  extra admitted-root cover pass, then removal of that pass when equivalent.
  **Check:** genuine hidden axioms, admission accounting and existing negative
  controls retain their decisions; sealed-field queries, complete census and
  recursive kernel checking retain their separate obligations. The current
  production prover and workaround stay in place until qualification passes.

The [performance contract](tools/performance.md) owns equivalent-work measurements.
Prioritize measured mutation-test and census-replay costs through their existing
owners. Optional model-build and live-session tools remain optional until their
own qualification and end-to-end measurements justify a change of default.

## Standard-tool guardrail replacements

The replacements are landed: typecheck passes indexed filenames to locked tools; uv checks locked resolution; authored registry/release identities generate views; `requires-python` selects the interpreter. The [generated rule reference](tools/check-rules.md) and [tool guide](tools/README.md#checking-the-tools-themselves) name owners and retained refusals. Retired IDs remain permanent.

Independent obligations remain active: annotation coverage and type analysis,
licence-review bindings, semantic document consistency and detection of pipe-led
runs without a table separator. Generation does not authorize a new reviewed
dependency version or bless a requirement/prose reading.

The `guardrails-20261030-repair` journal owns its predecessor revision's hosted evidence. Later batches require fresh Windows/Ubuntu Host CI and all Guest dispatches, without waiting for Guest verdicts.

### Declarative guardrail follow-through

Structural policy uses standard JSON Schema and existing dependency bindings, with no upgrade. Runtime validation and editors share schemas; semantic cross-artifact checks retain their owners.

- **Typechecker configuration policy. Start:** the settings validators in
  `vos/cli/typecheck.py`. **Owns:** authored schemas for the admitted ty and Ruff
  configuration shapes, validated after TOML decoding by the existing strict
  JSON Schema implementation. **Check:** preserve every admitted and refused
  reviewed setting, required exact-value table, unknown-key refusal, malformed-input
  refusal and report-beside-checker behavior. Diagnostic wording may use standard
  schema messages with configuration paths and group findings by violated schema
  predicate. Close unreviewed top-level ty keys, including diagnostic baselines,
  rather than allowing future tool-valid settings to suppress diagnostics. Keep
  suppression scanning, explicit
  indexed targets, both platform passes and platform-import checks active.
- **Maintenance data shapes. Start:** work-order and calibration readers and the
  embedded checker-registry schema. **Owns:** authored schemas and editor
  associations; remove handwritten structural validators where the schemas
  decide the same predicate. **Check:** preserve version and strict-integer
  refusals, exact keys, nonempty text, list uniqueness and class membership;
  retain duplicate-JSON-key rejection and every semantic leaf, join, containment,
  live-requirement and field-owner check. The generated artifacts remain byte
  identical. Focused negative controls cover malformed and missing schemas.
- **Reviewed Verilator binding. Start:** K-97 and the existing development-tool
  `assign` owner. **Owns:** a structured licence-review identity bound to
  `rtl.py`'s `VERILATOR_PIN`, a generated version in its documentation, and
  retirement of K-97 into the permanent inactive registry. **Check:** K-118
  refuses changed source or review identities, absent or ambiguous source
  assignments and stale generated documentation; moving a release still requires
  its licence reading. Retain historical holder citations and the current pin.

The integrator reads the settled change at Tier A, runs focused behavioral
controls and typecheck, requires Host CI on Windows and Ubuntu, and dispatches
every Guest CI lane for the published revision without waiting for Guest CI.

Implemented: shared configuration/maintenance schemas and editor support, with the review binding replacing K-97. Focused controls preserve predecessor decisions and generated bytes, and cover duplicate keys, strict integer lengths and offline schema-reference refusals. `guardrails-declarative-20261030-final` owns exact-revision Host evidence and all Guest dispatches.

## Parser and declaration guardrail consolidation

Replace handwritten syntax/data-shape readers with locked PyYAML and JSON Schema, preserving model/proof/compiler bytes, dependency releases and acceptance boundaries. These contracts precede implementation; the integrator reads settled changes at Tier A.

- **Register and format declarations. Start:** structural checks in

Focused behavioral controls and integrated typecheck provide local feedback.
Require Host CI on Windows and Ubuntu and dispatch every Guest CI lane for the
published revision, finishing without waiting for Guest CI. The batch's fanout
journal records the tested revision and hosted evidence; no performance claim or
milestone completion credit is taken.

Device-register/wire-format schemas serve readers and editors; semantic joins remain authored.   scanner policy and remaining boundary here.

Implemented: `vos/ring.py` owns parsing/emission and direct library imports; composition/ring share the lazy `vos/schema.py` validator. Source inventories bind new dependencies. The static-memory CLI injects registration, digest and replay dependencies into its manifest library.

`vos/prooflex.py` shares comment/string traversal. Adapters retain distinct policies: proof reading tolerates unfinished input and recognizes strings inside comments; navigation ignores comment quotes and refuses unmatched endings or unfinished regions. Frozen-lexer and proof-command comparisons, focused differential fixtures and live-source readings preserve both outputs. Require settled-revision Host CI and matching Guest dispatch; reuse an existing dispatch with identical inputs.

## Change-aware CI selection

**Contract:** select validation from the complete changed-path set, with one shared
classifier for hosted workflows and the publication handoff. Prose-only changes
retain the corpus checker and Markdown validation on Windows and Ubuntu, while
omitting behavioral/mutation shards, Python typechecking and unaffected model
hooks. Markdown consumed as executable configuration keeps its consuming gates.
Model changes select the model and RTL guest lanes, RTL changes select RTL, and
proof changes select proofs; shared tooling, CI configuration, unknown paths and
unavailable comparisons conservatively select the complete suites. Mixed changes
take the union. Manual full runs, cold installation and explicit proof-reading
acceptance remain available and cannot be narrowed by automatic classification.

**Acceptance:** exercise documentation, executable Markdown, each source family,
mixed changes, deleted/renamed files, unavailable history and full-run overrides.
Keep both Host CI aggregate names and reject failed, canceled or unexpectedly
skipped required jobs. Record the comparison and selected or omitted guest lanes
in the fanout journal; reuse a frozen selection on resume and dispatch only the
selected lanes after Host CI passes, without waiting for Guest CI. Preserve full
scheduled coverage and its existing successful-revision deduplication. Update
AGENTS.md and the workflow/handoff contracts together. Require passing Host CI on
the settled published revision and dispatch every guest lane for this change to
the CI mechanism itself.

**Execution:** Hosted selection and `fanout` share the classifier, with focused
scope, publication/resume and override controls plus static analysis passing.
[Full-source Host CI](https://github.com/symbiogenesis/VerifiedOS/actions/runs/41316405666) passed for
`6ddfd5dc8ab739f6271912f90d32fd7a40862c9d`; [Guest CI](https://github.com/symbiogenesis/VerifiedOS/actions/runs/41317609248) was dispatched
for the same revision with every lane, without awaiting its verdict. The
`ci-scope-20261101-final` journal owns the prose-only hosted control and
retirement of this session's lanes.

improvement and no material whole-run regression; retain rejected or inconclusive
comparisons explicitly. **Join:** passing Host CI on both platforms and every Guest
CI lane dispatched for the settled revision, without waiting for Guest CI.

**Execute:** use dedicated checker, census and runner worktrees against this landed
predicate. Prefer eliminating duplicate scans and quadratic joins before adding
concurrency. Scheduling changes retain complete populations and stable reporting;
skip an option whose resources or measured benefit do not justify adoption.
Existing Q35q and Q43 qualification boundaries and candidate/campaign allowances
remain unchanged. Record measured dispositions in the performance owner.

**State:** the selected checker, census, Markdown, repair and proof-context
implementations are integrated on `main`. The
[recorded comparisons](docs/performance/tool-benchmarks-2026-10-30/README.md)
accept the complete checker and proof-header runtime improvements, retain smaller
Markdown gains and inconclusive proof-context timings, and reject slower scanner
experiments. The [follow-through](#guardrail-performance-follow-through) owns the
subsequent bounded Markdown workers; other scheduling changes are not selected.
The native census comparison still needs the original three-root control emission;
its complete qualification remains open below. The stable-tree Host and Guest
handoff retains the check and join requirements above.

## Guardrail performance follow-through

**Start:** the [performance acceptance contract](tools/performance.md), the landed
guardrail algorithms above and their retained hosted profiles. **Owns:** an
ASCII-only specialization of the bounded name matcher with the complete Unicode
route retained; removal of repeated left-boundary work only where complete word
runs establish the same boundary; and bounded, independent-document Markdown
workers over immutable source and configuration snapshots. Existing checker,
mutation, proof and census acceptance populations remain complete.

**Check:** compare the matcher with its frozen oracle at every tested source
offset, window edge, prefix alternative and Unicode transition. Markdown must
validate exactly the selected documents, preserve ordered diagnostics and fresh
configuration reads, cap its worker population, and terminate failed or timed-out
workers before replaying the existing serial route. Exercise malformed inputs,
worker failures, omitted or duplicate results and edits between invocations.
Focused fixture checks may run locally; all measurements and complete suites run
on GitHub Actions. Paired hosted measurements bind identical inputs and tool
revisions, alternate repetitions, and record component and process elapsed time,
worker counts and sampled process-tree memory for concurrent work. Accept only
repeatable improvement without a material whole-run regression. Require Host CI
on both platforms and dispatch every Guest CI lane for the settled revision,
without waiting for Guest CI.

**State:** the matcher and bounded Markdown workers are integrated on `main`.
The [hosted follow-through records](docs/performance/tool-benchmarks-2026-10-30/README.md)
accept both complete-command runtime improvements with identical reports. The
Markdown comparison records its higher aggregate residency and slightly higher
total CPU alongside the worker cap. Proof-context freshness reads, native kernel
execution and native census qualification retain their existing contracts; no
additional acceptance cache is commissioned. The final Host and Guest handoff
retains the check requirements above.

## Native Sail census validation

Open under [Q35q](PLAN.md). Full native
census generation, Python validation and retained-evidence replay are
[GitHub-hosted Actions only](docs/implementation/contracts/sail-support-census.md#hosted-only-execution).
Target a tenfold reduction in end-to-end Python replay time without increasing
peak memory. No such speedup has been measured; an interrupted local run supplies
no completed baseline.

- Profile parsing, schema traversal, native metadata/resolver joins, control-copy
  cost and each corruption case on the exact hash-bound support and control
  emissions. Record phase and end-to-end wall time, peak RSS and temporary disk
  use, with runner image, interpreter, source revision and repetitions.
- Investigate compiling the checked schema once per invocation, resolving schema
  references in advance, dispatching constructor branches by their discriminators,
  and building diagnostic paths only on failure. Preserve schema vocabulary
  refusal, all compatible `oneOf`/`anyOf` branches and every existing constraint.
- Reduce redundant parsing, encoding and traversal within an invocation. Preserve
  independent artifact replay, isolated control mutations and complete per-case
  schema/graph validation; introduce no cross-call acceptance cache or omitted
  resolver effect. Differential fixtures must cover malformed as well as valid
  inputs before a replacement reader is qualified.
- Evaluate bounded hosted jobs for the control cases, joining exact source/input
  hashes and the complete case population. Keep each job below the workflow's
  runtime and measured memory/disk budgets; duplicating a large parsed graph across
  concurrent workers needs its own resource measurements. Job partitioning alone
  establishes no reduction in total validation work.

Acceptance compares equivalent complete work on the same hosted runner image and
input bytes. Preserve the full support census, positive control plus all twelve
independently validated corruptions, exact assumptions, recursive kernel joins and
unchanged-input checks. Record the actual speedup and resource results under [the performance rules](tools/performance.md); retain any baseline gap or missed target explicitly.
This follow-up changes neither Q35q's acceptance nor its candidate/campaign allowances.

## Proof and specification proposals

These proposals retain the [proof map](docs/assurance/unassigned-proof-map.md)'s obligation endpoints and transfer analysis. Commissioned aliases point to their
existing priced owners; other ranges remain prospective and outside the priced
implementation total. No proposal adds a dependency to M8a. Numbered inventory
rows and map-section references in these proposals and the mandatory-work list
refer to the proof map and its linked inventory, rather than this plan's component
sections.

Each slice carries its class and range on the plan's conventions, its kind, and the four dispatch clauses. None is on the M8a roster and none adds a dependency to it; a slice opens on its Start and never on its position here.

- **U-01 · Author the security policy model** · 32 h, range 18–46 · X · `S`. **Start:** R-08-024 through R-08-028, R-17-012, R-05-156a, R-05-156b, R-08-027a, R-08-027c, ApexTheorem.v's `Vocabulary`, HandlerGraph.v's typed graph. **Owns:** one file under `proofs/` instantiating `Policy`, `indist`, `Declass`, `D` and the three `release_*` quotients over a compartment type read from the composed graph and an input type carrying content and cycle-level arrival timing, stating `explicit_flow_noninterference` and `declassified_flows_authorized` as theorem targets, the device-observation clause as a named definition, and each R-05-156b proviso proved as a lemma or refuted by a constructed instance. **Check:** `python tools/run.py proofs` reports the file's constant count with an empty assumption set; two witnesses land, one where a release bound wider than the user named is refused (R-17-012's leak) and one where the named grant flows; a refuted proviso names the register amendment it owes; both field-binding rows stay `none yet`; K-109 binds the header. **Join:** U-25 reads the isolation predicate; the row flips to `authored` only at its R-05-150 review, Tier A.
- **U-02 · Author the NoC and island isolation model** · 30 h, range 16–44 · X · `S`. **Start:** R-15-211, R-15-222, R-15-222a, R-15-223, R-15-228, R-15-228d, R-17-003b through R-17-003d, U-03's arbiter relation, U-05's schedule record, M0.14's class constants, Q22e's verdict on the store-buffer arm. **Owns:** one file under `proofs/` stating `timing_isolation` as a 2-safety hyperproperty over a timing observation of one partition's own accesses, parametric over the arbiter relation, the slot table and the latency constants, quantifying over the flush at switches, the per-island arbitration, the per-core rotation, the link's slot table and the socket's transfer slots, with the shared-macro periphery named as the physical residual it does not carry. **Check:** the assumption audit is empty; a work-conserving arbiter, a slot reassignment, a data-dependent grant and a link landing that depends on a peer's readiness are each refuted instances; Q23f's composed statement compiles against it; K-109. **Join:** seams 1 and 2 read the field; the row flips at review, Tier A.
- **U-03 · Put the TDM NoC arbitration and the per-island memory arbitration in the Sail model** · 18 h, range 12–24 · I · `S`. **Start:** R-15-211, R-15-228, R-15-222a, R-15-212, R-11-017's slot table, M0.14 and M0.16, Q22e's `phase-service` contract seam. **Owns:** the arbiter's transition relation and the slot table as composition declarations in `model/`, refused where malformed, with no requester-visible window beyond the schedule's, and the mapping of the emitted schedule into `phase-service --contract`. **Check:** `python tools/run.py model build` green; seeded refusals fire for a work-conserving grant, a slot with two tenants and a grant that reads occupancy; the differential corpus gains one member whose digest is unmoved when an unrelated island's load changes; `git ls-files --eol` reads `i/lf w/lf` on every touched file. **Join:** U-02, the NoC block after U-24, Q22e's instance.

## Mandatory work outside the priced total

Each item below is owed by the register and reached by no slice above. Where the plan names an owner the owner is stated; where it names none, the act that would name one is stated instead. None of it is zero.

- **The Iris-over-Sail logic's establishment**, R-13-017's four theories with the Katamaran and μSail agreement theorem: the hardening owner Q2c's `machine-logic` key names. U-22's reviewed breakdown has landed; Q35m/Q35k/Q35l price the bounded dependency trial and conditional unary/byte increments. Required selected-subset agreement must be scoped, priced and landed before Q35k; full agreement and the remaining theories stay unpriced and require their own implementation scope and estimate before construction.
- **The remaining route (a) refinements**: the DMA fabric and the TDM NoC block after U-24, the remaining fixed-function sequencers, Q23g's endpoint theorem beyond its predicate, the hyperproperty half of every block, and the FEV evidence R2 and R3 owe the imported cores. **The RoT core and the crypto core's masked datapath have no authoring-route entry**; R1c records the first as a register gap and this map records the second, and neither block's refinement or countermeasure can be assigned before that act.
- **The robust-preservation proof** over the backend, per pass, and its preservation through M1.3 and M1.3a's optimizations: M1.2's children own the backend and exclude the proof; priced at entry after M1.2f and U-22.
- **The R-05-023a producer, the per-image records, R-07-050's fast-path proof and R-05-026's correspondence theorem**: M1.4 and U-26 decide the instrument; construction is priced at entry.
- **The constant-time metatheorem's proof and every per-binary proof**, and with them **CHERI-TAL soundness** (`CJ-TAL-SOUND`, R-18-018's second sub-deliverable), which carries no cell, and the Binsec/Rel bring-up gate.
- **The WCET toolchain pass** (R-18-024's second sub-deliverable), which carries no cell, the on-device integration on M6.2b-ii's route, and the soundness proof.
- **Row 2's flow theorem** over the kernel and the robust-declassification proof: the kernel's refinement (R-07-001, R-08-022), for which M4.4 prices bring-up alone.
- **R-15-108's design-space exploration**, which fixes row 9's values, row 15's pipeline depths and the TDM schedule, and sits outside the grand total by the plan's own statement.
- **Row 13's field population** at R5, **row 15's silicon magnitudes** at R5, M9 and M9a, and **M3.6b's** invariance measurement.
- **Row 10's remaining descriptors**, the ASN.1 to Narcissus front end (R-18-029), the NAS grammar with its differential corpus (R-05-050), each descriptor's copy-once Clight lowering (R-05-043, Q2b's route for one), and each canonicity theorem: priced per descriptor at entry after U-14.
- **Row 25's interpreter**, its GC-free lowering, its refinement proof, the robust-confinement proof and the verified binary parser.
- **Rows 19 through 21's models and row 22's remaining L2CAP/GATT procedures and timers**, priced at entry after U-18's pairing subset, and **all four sequencer refinements** with their Lustre nodes on M6.1b's route.
- **The R-16-008f theorem on the final binary**, the compiler-emitted signature instrumentation, which no toolchain cell owns, the countermeasure construction, and the R-17-058d reduction the post-M10 cell prices over accepted premises.
- **The TLS implementation** for M6.9a and **the HAL implementation** for row 18, each in certifying Rust (R-05-084), and with them **R-18-014's certifying Rust compiler**, whose remaining functional adaptation and certificate obligations carry no cell. Before pricing that compiler, its requirement owner records [the shared-lowering future review](docs/background/architectural-alternatives.md#sharing-verified-lowering-before-a-second-native-compiler-future-review): backend reuse with the existing safe-Rust preservation/certificate promise, and the separate feature decision of replacing Rust-assigned consumers with a checked GC-free Gallina/C route. Every current compiler and consumer obligation remains required until a reviewed register act changes it; no saving or proof status is inferred from the comparison.
- **The certifying-Rust certificate mode and the full Iris refinement proofs** for the kernel and the storage stack, the hardening program the post-M10 opening names.

## Security-policy realization and review

U-01's [authored model and proposed acceptance predicate](docs/assurance/security-policy-model.md#10-acceptance-predicate-and-remaining-work)
remain subject to independent review. The following open work connects that model
to the implementation and its proofs. These existing obligations retain their
owners and acceptance boundaries; this list creates no additional priced cells
or completion credit.

| Remaining work | Owner and required result |
| --- | --- |
| Graph and object realization | Composition/kernel work: derive labels and channels from the admitted graph, bind object identities and rights to CHERI bounds, and bind the total site space to repeated external events |
| Complete observations | R-08-027a through R-08-027c: bind the snapshot observation fields to full histories and the actual scheduler, termination and restart transitions; establish schedule-only observable progress |
| Consent and lifecycle realization | Powerbox, trusted path and storage work: authenticated live/recorded witness production, freshness, request gating, one-shot consumption, revocation, cuts, record compatibility and unique act-instance indexing |
| Full robust-strategy theorem | R-08-025 and R-08-022: prove the strategy/input correspondence, including permitted revoke-only actions, over the kernel and multikernel semantics |
| Runtime arrival release | Register owner: decide the timing scope, then implement and review the selected runtime relation |
| Unconditional apex use | Apex integration: discharge or replace extensionality premises and bind actual D to the compared executions, without `True` placeholders for other workstreams |
| Compiler preservation | `CJ-SECOMP`: state and prove robust preservation against the reviewed isolation model |
| Independent review | R-05-150 and R-08-028: review both artifacts at the same edition and set crown-jewel row 2's status |

## Language core proposals

Source design and missing-law analysis remain in [the core design](docs/languages/vela/core-design.md#proof-obligations-and-proposed-work-packages).

The [Vela whole-system proposal](#vela-whole-system-authoring-proposal) extends the intended authoring surface to general proofs, the ISA model and hardware. The bounded packages below retain their own scope and estimates; none includes that extension's full establishment or migration work.

The following owner keys are proposals, not schedule IDs or proof constants. Each selected package needs a reviewed checklist cell and entry predicate before implementation dispatch. Estimates are prospective attended-work design judgments, not measured agent-session results or conversions of the strategy's person-month bands. They exclude the unbounded foundation work and therefore do not establish a complete implementation price. Keep eventual human attended work, summed agent-session wall time and machine time in separate ledgers under Q20a. The general-logic and machine connections remain [Q2c's prerequisites](PLAN.md#q-assessment-actions); its rejection supplies neither an implementation budget nor a law.

| Proposed owner | Estimate | Work and acceptance boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `core-soundness` | 160 h, range 80-240 | Define judgments/interpreter and prove substitution, phase separation, preservation, loan/exit restoration and kinded authority/effect composition for this fragment over actual supplied logic laws. Excludes concrete CHERI primitive discovery, frontend and backend. |
| `core-frontend` | 140 h, range 80-200 | Restricted parsing/resolution, bounded flow analysis, reconstructed arithmetic, checked elaboration/SourcePackage correspondence and erasure preservation. Excludes general dependent inference, effect polymorphism, kernel, backend and editor. |
| `core-diagnostics` | 64 h, range 40-88 | Adapt the qualified interaction path to frontend derivations, typed holes, status and invalidation; demonstrate the specified source residuals. Excludes Q19a's existing-tool qualification and a new language server. |
| `core-repair-trial` | 48 h, range 32-64 | Author and qualify the combined fixture/negative neighbors and its concrete `PreparationOpen`/`finish` interpretation using the supplied resource laws, then measure semantic/refactor/representation replay and review. Excludes discovering those laws, a repair service and Q20's held-out staging adaptation. |

### Vela whole-system authoring proposal

The [pure translation prototype](docs/languages/vela/vela-prototype.md) is Q25e's
separately commissioned first executable tool; Q25f aligns it with the proposed
source surface. Its Nat/bool fragment denotes
generated CIC terms and exercises parsing, explicit checking and Gallina emission.
The selected development parser goal is
[Menhir generation](docs/languages/vela/verification-strategy.md#development-parser-and-grammar),
with a reviewed grammar of the proposed surface, located syntax trees and separate
elaboration. The `vela-parser` row below owns qualification and migration from the
handwritten prototype. Pinned-kernel replay of emitted definitions and independently
expected evaluations, followed by checked source correspondence, remain additional
work. Each increment needs its own reviewed cell and estimate before dispatch.
The full-language frontend retains Q25d's admission prerequisites.

The design goal is one authored Vela codebase for operational software, independently reviewed contracts, mathematical proofs, the architectural machine model and hardware descriptions. The [unified-source contract](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md) owns the technical acceptance boundary. Generated Clight, Rocq proof terms, executable simulators, binaries and RTL remain permitted; the software golden artifact is the proof-connected production implementation. A separately maintained optimized rewrite is outside this goal.

Vela also targets a [native, verified, self-hosting compiler](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#native-verified-compiler-and-self-hosting), authored in Vela and using its own intermediate representations to reach exact target bytes without a mandatory Clight/CompCert or LLVM stage. Reusable transformation proofs compose during compilation; independent kernel checking, verified bootstrap and final-artifact correspondence remain required. Competitive target optimization and commercial use, redistribution and resident rebuilding of the complete dependency closure need their own qualification. Interim compiler bridges remain valid while that endpoint is unbuilt.

The intended toolchain includes [LSP editor services, DAP source debugging and
DWARF native debug information](docs/languages/vela/tooling-protocols.md). The
separate stages below own their protocol and consumer qualification. They reuse
parser/frontend, contextual-diagnostic and backend work at its existing owners;
the `core-diagnostics` estimate excludes a new server. Semantic editor features
retain the frontend hold, while syntax-only development support can be scoped
earlier. Native debugging waits for its executable backend and exact-image debug
maps. The current prototypes supply none of these interfaces.

The [metaprogramming contract](docs/languages/vela/metaprogramming.md) adds ordinary
static computation, opt-in structural descriptions and contextual checked
generation to the intended surface. `vela-meta` first qualifies a scoped static
evaluation slice, then descriptor/derivation and cross-feature composition slices;
runtime-cost acceptance additionally needs the selected backend. Public semantic
snapshots belong to `vela-compiler-services`, shared with generation and LSP
clients. Q25m specifies these boundaries only. Q25k's mathematical prototype,
Q25i's accepted grammar and the existing proposal estimates fund no additional
implementation. Each selected slice needs its own priced entry and acceptance
predicate; source and final-artifact qualification remain distinct.

Q25g and Q25h commission the bounded parser increments in the table; Q25i commissions the wider syntax portion of core-frontend. The other proposal owner keys have establishment and maintenance costs **unpriced**. They are not checklist IDs, checked artifacts or additions to the priced totals. Before implementation, split a selected stage into reviewed checklist cells with its own budget and entry predicate, charge shared foundation work once, and take the required register/prose acts. The existing compiler, machine-logic and hardware work retains its accepted subjects until a successor passes the migration gate. AI may propose programs, proofs and optimizations; acceptance still requires checked evidence and independent review of intended behavior.

| Proposed owner and order | Start | Owns | Check | Join |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `vela-parser` · Menhir during development | Q25f's pure source baseline and the [bounded qualification and migration contract](docs/languages/vela/vela-parser.md); Q25g commissions the pure syntax increment, Q25i the full development surface, and Q25h checked parsing and Python retirement | Q25g and Q25h own their separately priced qualification; broader parser construction stays in `core-frontend`'s existing scope and is charged once | The [development parser contract](docs/languages/vela/verification-strategy.md#development-parser-and-grammar), refined by Q25g/Q25h/Q25i's acceptance predicates; current compatibility and licensing records are inputs, not acceptance | The generated parser replaces Python parsing only after Q25h's migration predicates pass; full frontend admission retains Q25d's prerequisites, and Vela-authored parsing and retirement of seed/development dependencies remain `vela-native`/`vela-bootstrap` duties |
| `vela-meta` · staged evaluation and generation | A separately scoped interpretation and required-static interface, reviewed descriptor/construction protocol and the existing frontend-release predicates | Static evaluation, type/declaration descriptions, contextual hygienic derivation and evidence generation; separately qualify the selected dependent/graded/resource/effect interactions | [Metaprogramming qualification](docs/languages/vela/metaprogramming.md#qualification): independent codec intent and refusals, static/runtime separation, privacy/coherence, target semantics, stale dependency rejection and runtime-artifact cost evidence | Reuse `core-soundness`, frontend, diagnostics and compiler duties at the [composition joins](docs/languages/vela/full-language-review.md#metaprogramming-and-compiler-service-extension); no new kernel conversion or admission path, and no implicit budget extension |
| `vela-compiler-services` · shared source services | A separately priced supported subset of located syntax, resolution, typing/elaboration and dependency identities; semantic operations retain their frontend prerequisites | Versioned immutable source/semantic snapshots, diagnostics, generated-source provenance, analysis/refactoring and checked generation requests for library and editor clients | [Compiler-service boundary](docs/languages/vela/metaprogramming.md#compiler-services-and-runtime-information) and [qualification](docs/languages/vela/metaprogramming.md#qualification): shared resolved identities, incomplete-input separation, stale results/edits and generated-source mapping | Compiler owners implement semantics once; `core-diagnostics` supplies judgments and `vela-lsp` supplies the protocol adapter; no dynamic native loading or independent proof acceptance |
| `vela-lsp` · editor services | A separately priced supported subset of compiler source services and the [LSP contract](docs/languages/vela/tooling-protocols.md#language-server-protocol); semantic/proof features require their qualified frontend/diagnostic producers | LSP lifecycle, overlays, navigation, diagnostics, source edits and optional versioned proof-state presentation; consume `core-diagnostics` judgments without duplicating them | [Protocol qualification](docs/languages/vela/tooling-protocols.md#qualification-and-delivery), including two clients, Unicode positions, dependency invalidation, stale replies and refusal of interactive proof acceptance | Compiler-owned source identities and batch-only evidence; independent of the retained Rocq-editor qualification |
| `vela-dwarf` · native debug information | An executable producer with a fixed ABI, compiler source maps and the [DWARF contract](docs/languages/vela/tooling-protocols.md#dwarf-native-debug-information) | Emit and preserve source/type/scope/location/unwind data through final composition; exact-image sidecars, CHERI encodings and consumer compatibility | [Protocol qualification](docs/languages/vela/tooling-protocols.md#qualification-and-delivery), including independent reading, optimized locations, capability values, malformed sections and source/image mismatch | Native lowering and final-layout owners supply mappings once; metadata remains outside execution SRAM and the install path |
| `vela-dap` · source debugger | A separately qualified execution backend, matching source/debug artifacts and the [DAP contract](docs/languages/vela/tooling-protocols.md#debug-adapter-protocol); physical targets additionally require authorized development/RMA access | DAP lifecycle, source stops/stepping, stacks/scopes/values and bounded read-only evaluation; simulator first, explicit backend capabilities | [Protocol qualification](docs/languages/vela/tooling-protocols.md#qualification-and-delivery), including independently expected stops/values, stale handles, unauthorized inspection and production-target refusal | Consume `vela-dwarf` for native images; preserve hardware trigger exclusion and immutable code, without charging backend construction to the adapter |
| `vela-software` · first software bridge | Q25d's frontend-release conditions and the actual CHERI laws required by the selected component | Vela source/contract correspondence and explicit-memory lowering through the retained compiler route | [Software acceptance](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#software-and-proof-boundary), including error paths, source edits and exact installed bytes | Current compiler/admission and canonical Sail consumers; no handwritten optimized implementation for the accepted family |
| `vela-proofs` · complete proof authorship | The interpreted frontend and reviewed theorem subjects for a selected library or metatheory package | General mathematical definitions and proofs in Vela, interoperating with retained checked libraries | [Proof-surface acceptance](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#software-and-proof-boundary); no mandatory handwritten Rocq fallback for the accepted package | Rocq/CIC rechecks generated terms and exact assumptions; replacing its checker is a separate decision |
| `vela-native` · native verified compiler | Admitted source/IR interpretations, concrete CHERI laws, selected target and reviewed replacement-duty scope; independent of machine-authoring migration | Vela-authored native pipeline, representation and optimization proofs, selection/allocation, ABI, assembly/link/image construction and complete licence closure | [Native-compiler acceptance](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#native-verified-compiler-and-self-hosting), including held-out systems/compute clients, checked search proposals, relational security, resources and final bytes | Current producers and validation remain required until equivalent evidence and explicit amendments admit the replacement; no assumed LLVM parity or performance saving |
| `vela-bootstrap` · verified self-hosting | Qualified native compiler definition, sufficient Vela implementation/proof surface and a reviewed seed/evaluation trust boundary | Connection from proved compiler definition to running binary, exact bootstrap stages and checked self-compilation of its declared source closure | [Bootstrap acceptance](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#native-verified-compiler-and-self-hosting); source/binary correspondence and assumptions check, with reproducibility additional to the theorem | Resident-toolchain runtime/admission and distribution duties; independent evidence checking remains required |
| `vela-machine` · qualify the ISA replacement | An admitted interpretation, independently reviewed machine contract and the pinned Sail model | Vela-authored machine definition, parameterized configuration closure, executable semantics and migration agreement | [Machine-model acceptance](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#machine-model) and its [negative cases](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#qualification-cases) | Qualification replays binary logic, TAL, constant-time, timing and hardware evidence over one candidate term; accepted-generation consumers switch at adoption |
| `vela-hardware` · hardware authoring and generation | The hardware subset's reviewed cycle/register interpretation and selected block contract; machine adoption need not wait for every hardware port | Vela hardware descriptions and checked generation/refinement for each selected block | [Hardware acceptance](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#hardware), preserving the block's required evidence or theorem tier | Retained RTL and imported-core paths through explicit interfaces; the final whole-system authoring claim needs every in-scope authored block |
| `vela-retirement` · canonical-model adoption and retirement | Accepted machine agreement, reviewed amendment dossier and completed affected-consumer inventory | Atomic adoption of the shared replacement model and retirement of active Sail authoring/tool dependencies | [Migration and retirement](docs/languages/vela/unified-source-contract.md#migration-and-retirement); no private semantics, circular oracle or missing consumer evidence | Updated register/prose and all affected compiler, admission, proof, emulator, timing and hardware artifacts in the same admitted generation |

The proof surface, native compiler and hardware subset may be qualified independently once their own inputs exist; verified self-hosting consumes the qualified native compiler. Machine adoption is a separate decision from hardware-source migration: both sides of the retained hardware theorem must name the newly admitted canonical model before Sail retires, even when the hardware description is still authored through the current route. Historical Sail evidence remains retained. Complete single-language authorship additionally requires replacing every in-scope handwritten implementation and proof surface and, for the declared toolchain closure, qualifying the native compiler and bootstrap; a successful compiler frontend alone closes none of those claims.

## Machine-logic compatibility continuation

The [dependency trial](docs/assurance/machine-logic-dependency-trial.md) records the accepted bounded trial and its failures.

The [opam compatibility policy](tools/opam/README.md) permits an immutable
source route with bounded reviewed patches at the locked versions, so a release
publication is no longer a prerequisite for this continuation. The
[Sail support package trial](tools/opam/sail-compatibility.md) demonstrates
that route's build boundary, but qualifies no Islaris, Lithium, Katamaran,
RecordUpdate or Equations input. Keep Rocq 9.3.0 and the current dependency
versions; no library downgrade, warning suppression or additional assumption
is authorized by this policy.

The machine-logic foundation owner and opam carriage owner must review and price
one source-compatibility continuation before dispatch. Its identified work is:

- Resolve the namespace deprecations in the exact Islaris and Lithium sources,
  the postfix-notation diagnostics in exact RecordUpdate and Katamaran, and the
  source-level pinned-setting conflicts, keeping the compiler settings fixed.
- Select the [merged upstream import cleanup](docs/assurance/machine-logic-dependency-trial.md#the-programequality-imports-separate-assumption-conflict), verify the absence of its
  loaded `eq_rect_eq` axiom in the complete candidate closure, and select the
  remaining Equations, stdpp-unstable/bitvector and isla-lang generation inputs
  with their actual licences and exact identities. No API port depth is measured
  by the dependency trial's early failures, so deeper API work is neither asserted necessary
  nor included in a numerical estimate.
- Establish the frozen elaborated readings required by the
  [proof-assistance workflow](docs/assurance/proof-assistance.md#statement-freeze), compile both
  complete selected closures under unchanged strict settings, enumerate their
  exact assumptions and read their recursive kernel summaries. Any changed
  frozen meaning returns to its owner instead of being accepted as a repair.
- Decide carriage separately and take fresh hosted acceptance over the settled
  inputs. Only an accepted compatible/carried result opens Q35k.

This is unpriced source-compatibility work, not an extension of Q35m's bounded
trial estimate. It authorizes no shared-switch change, framework replacement,
semantic weakening, μSail translation or new assurance assumption.

   RTL refinement.

## Assessment follow-through

The [critique](docs/background/critique.md) owns the findings and dispositions.
The permanent critique ID below is a follow-up within Q22e, with no additional
estimate or independent completion claim.

10. **Resolve the store buffer against phase service.** Q22e owns the comparison; [the alternatives entry](docs/background/architectural-alternatives.md#delete-the-store-buffer-sequential-consistency-by-absence-distinguished-from-buffer-retained-sc) owns its disposition and symbol table.

    Zero-wait deletion for arbitrary admitted code is ruled out on every second-class bank (refresh grants no requester service) and every multi-domain island. A single-domain first-class island qualifies only if bank occupancy is one cycle (R-17-041's crown-jewel residual), or checked code/schedule evidence forbids same-bank requests within that occupancy; the restricted companions still lack that certificate.

    The synthetic assessment covers average-rate grant gaps, joint acceptance, refresh, frame wrap, one hart's acceptance order across unequal fabric paths, and an occupied bank on arrival. `phase-evaluate` joins schedule extraction, conservative cost arithmetic and completion analysis separating in-flight-to-bank-acceptance time from total issued-operation drain. Receipts derive their open verdict from composition qualification/freeze flags and the schedule, whose producer artifact is absent.

    The open cost branch compares bounded issue stalls with switch savings. Required coefficients remain R-11-017's emitted schedule, R-15-108's widths, R-15-228's binding map and R-15-052b roster, R-15-247m's qualification, R-07-032/R-11-006's switch count, and R-17-041's unfrozen switch constants. The second-class comparison uses R-15-015b's ordered unbuffered baseline and R-15-218's SRAM-only drain. It still needs qualified routing, grants, occupancy and permitted refresh phases under R-15-247m, plus R-07-040's complete timer residency under the actual schedule; no finite bound means refusal.

    The synthetic `phase-stall` instrument bounds issue waiting over declared per-hart programs and joins cost arithmetic. Its mode-transition extension checks modes/transitions as one reachable product. Both retain every coefficient as owed. Keep the Ztso baseline until the cost branch is decided.

## Upstream adoption recommendations

The [merged-upstream review](docs/implementation/upstream-merged-review.md) owns dated source findings and local-consumer evidence. These actions retain existing implementation owners, qualification boundaries and tracker scope; they add no submission or implementation allowance.

### Recommended order

1. Port the Sail vector legality fix; qualify repaired Wasm numeric fixtures before freezing the interpreter corpus.
2. Trial CHERIoT Rust's existing calling conventions/attributes and newer pointer-width GVN correction for compiler adaptation; [the donor survey](docs/assurance/proof-reuse/languages.md#cheriot-rust-selected-functional-capability-aware-compiler-baseline) inventories them.
3. Trial Sail dependent-pair emission/configuration refusal at Q35, and coherent Stdlib-bits APIs before more retired-interface glue. Katamaran supplies a method reference across a different protection boundary.
4. Qualify assertion enablement/locking in the real-core harness; evaluate Mocha AXI collection at fabric binding.
5. Include Rocq inspection/checking changes in existing prover qualification; retain trust boundaries for optional certificate/compiler-pass experiments.

### Model and hardware

| Evidence | Next action and boundary |
| --- | --- |
| [Indexed segment-store overlap legality](docs/implementation/upstream-merged-review.md#model-and-hardware) | Port the segment-aware helper and `nf` under the Sail workflow. Adapt positive/negative cases to the frozen profile, preserving capability authorization and fault order. Q35/model reconciliation owns acceptance. |
| [A smaller vector-length representation](docs/implementation/upstream-merged-review.md#model-and-hardware) | Trial `vl`, prove representation agreement and zero-extend at CSR/external interfaces. Preserve the intentional `vstart` exclusion; wholesale register-block import would change the machine. |
| [Assertion controls that cannot be switched off by the design](docs/implementation/upstream-merged-review.md#model-and-hardware) | R2: compile retained assertions with `--assert`, lock runtime controls, retain a failing property and an attempted disable control. Reconcile imported assertion expectations before enablement. |
| [AXI request, response and completion observation](docs/implementation/upstream-merged-review.md#model-and-hardware) | At R1c-ii fabric binding, compare donor UVM analysis ports before new collection; qualify simulator compatibility or adapt their collection/protocol design to Verilator. Add capability tags, delegated-window identity, reset/cancellation and landed-write/window-close semantics. AXI response alone proves neither persistence nor revocation completion. |
| [Failing explicitly on unlowered formal checks](docs/implementation/upstream-merged-review.md#model-and-hardware) | Include this refusal in formal-frontend minimum-feature/regression qualification, retaining separate property-preservation checks. |
| [Concurrency-interface v2](docs/implementation/upstream-merged-review.md#model-and-hardware) | Review backend, runtime and outcome interfaces together. Hard-coded `CHERI=false`/`cap_size_log=0` TODOs leave candidate 16's granule-keyed tags unresolved. Preserve the canonical machine anchor and prove adapter data/tag effects before adoption. The date is the landed commit date. |

### Consume upstream and contribute the portable remainder

| Comparison seam | Consumption and possible contribution |
| --- | --- |
| Sail emitter and support library | Consume emitter capabilities while retaining strict syntax, notation and exact assumptions. Candidates 17/18 own portable source/backend changes; 16 owns tag outcomes. Coordinate rather than duplicate proposals or weaken the local term. |
| Curated vector semantics | Consume segment-overlap legality while preserving frozen CHERI fault order. Candidate 33 owns legality-before-assertion. Separate portable regressions from capability semantics absent in the donor. |
| Native proof inspection | At qualified adoption, replace only equivalent workaround behavior with merged checker/module printing. Candidates 4/10/19/22/32 own related changes. Retain stronger census/sealed-field checks until equivalence; contribute reduced regressions for residual generic defects. |
| Fiat-Crypto archive builds | Consume generator/proofs, retaining the [local source/index refusal](docs/implementation/fiat-crypto-emission.md#current-2026-10-19-emission-and-replay). Candidate 45 proposes complete archive imports or explicit refusal; contribute that behavior while keeping the generator. |
| Rust ABI, MMIO and shared objects | Start from selected donor mechanisms. For generic layout, tag-preservation or permission defects, produce reduced Rust/Rust or Rust/C regressions and coordinated fixes. Candidates 12/13 own known ABI/CTFE gaps; this survey establishes no further local implementation advantage. |
| Hardware and compute restrictions | Cache absence, all-M-mode capabilities, bespoke format and excluded subgroup/JIT surfaces are intentional adaptations. Deleting donor features is no generic improvement; contribute portable AXI/lowering corrections only when comparison demonstrates them. |
